| 2026-10-20 | W-20 builder's choices inside the coordinator's scope. (1) **PDFs keep the W-17 rule:** a `%PDF-` start is read as a PDF only under `application/pdf` or a missing or generic type; under an explicit text type it stays text and gains `type_mismatch`; under any other type it is a `file_warning` of kind `unknown` with `type_mismatch`. A declared `application/pdf` with a different signature (a program) is that kind. (2) **Office is always high risk** in v0.5: nothing inside a zip or OLE file is read, so macros cannot be ruled out. Inside the zip and OLE families the kind comes from the declared name or type (Office, add-on, or program for `.apk`, `.jar`, `.msi` and the like), since nothing inside is read; a Chrome `Cr24` file is an add-on by its mark. (3) **Only reads hash files.** read, check, watch, resume and a form's answer pass the file option (a read a 200, a form's answer any 2xx); robots.txt, discovery probes, `doors`, the Safe Browsing list and a form page name the response but keep its body as text exactly as before v0.5, under their own caps (Codex round 58: a robots.txt that starts `MZ` is still parsed as rules, never read as "no rules"); a form page that is a file stays `not_html`. (4) For a read, a page is held to the smaller of the page cap and the file cap (with the defaults, 5 MB; Codex round 65). A response the site says is over the file cap is `kind: "unknown"` (nothing was seen), `size: 0`; a page type that size fails as too large, as before. A file whose download runs out of time is a warning without a fingerprint, its kind known from its first bytes. (5) A file warning is never cached, and a cached page at a URL that now serves a file is removed (outside private mode). (6) **The strictest text rule wins** (Codex rounds 58 and 59): every rule the content, the declared type and both names ask for is collected, and the strictest applies: shortcut (never read), then CSV (formulas counted), then script, SVG, calendar, contact, and only then the declared HTML, JSON or text type. A `.mobileconfig` served as `text/html` is a shortcut, a `#!` line does not hide a `[Desktop Entry]` after it, and a `text/csv` body named `x.js` is read as CSV. A cached copy's fingerprint covers how it was read as well as its bytes, so the same bytes under a new kind are read again with their own flags. Binary means a NUL byte in the first 40 KB, or for UTF-16 with a byte-order mark a NUL character once decoded; a declared charset alone proves nothing; content checks (shortcut marks, `<html`) decode UTF-16 by its mark. A file a person saved is classified from its first 40 KB and then, if it is a file, hashed piece by piece up to the file cap like a download (only a page is held to the page cap). Scripts, CSV, calendar and contact files are read as plain text, never as markdown (a `# comment` is not a heading); parser version 9. (7) Names: `Content-Disposition` (`filename*` first, `filename` too when it differs: the strictest rule of all names applies) and the final URL's last segment; a name over 255 characters keeps its end; server route extensions (`.php`, `.aspx`, `.jsp` and the like) claim nothing; `double_extension` needs the inner extension to be a known file type, so `jquery.min.js` is not flagged. A missing type with a `.html`, `.json` or `.txt` name is read as that, as W-06 already did for saved files; a generic binary type with nothing that says text is `unknown`. (8) `pdf_launch` is found by scanning the PDF's plain bytes for a `/Launch` name (`#hh` spellings decoded), because pdf.js does not report Launch actions; one inside a compressed object stream is not seen. An open action that only goes to a page is not flagged. | The smallest safe reading of the scope: every guess goes toward "flag it", nothing new is parsed, and nothing that worked before breaks. |
| 2026-10-20 | W-21 builder's choices. (1) The command's program must be a **full path** (no `PATH` lookup, no `..`) and is checked to exist when it is set; at most 31 arguments. (2) It is started through a small runner (`hookrunner.ts`, node itself, no shell) that leads its **own process group**, keeps the hook's output (at most 4 KB) and answers Tiresias in one line, and kills the whole group with SIGKILL after answering, at the time cap, or within 200 ms of Tiresias being gone, however it stopped (Codex rounds 58 and 63); Tiresias also kills the group at the cap, on cancellation and once it has the answer, so a hook that starts helpers leaves nothing running. It inherits Tiresias's environment (it is the owner's own program). (3) It runs only on a high-risk warning **with a fingerprint** (a file over the cap, cut short or not downloaded is not scanned), and in private mode it still runs, with the fingerprint only. (4) Unknown keys in the output object are ignored; `verdict` must be one of the three and `note`, if present, a string (cut at 500). (5) With `send_file`, a quarantine copy that cannot be written means `verdict: "failed"` and the hook is not run. Copies are deleted right after the hook and, as a backup, when the call ends; `clear` and the daily trim delete copies older than 10 minutes left by a process that died. (6) A scanner setting that does not validate never stops a read, a resume, the daily trim or `clear` (the storage-limit loader ignores the key; Codex round 58): file warnings say `failed` by `config.json`, and `scanner show` shows the problem. (7) **No scanner on Windows** (Codex round 59): Windows has no process groups to kill, so a hook there could outlive its cap; the scanner is never started and every scan is `failed`. | Owner-only, no shell, nothing left running, nothing kept: the hook is a slot the owner fills, and an agent can neither reach nor skip it. |
| 2026-10-20 | Every version in order before launch: v0.6 inside files, v0.7 JavaScript-only pages (W-10), v0.8 tamper-proof receipts (W-08), v0.9 hardening and a private launch rehearsal, v1.0 public launch; payments at v1.1 (W-07). | Travis: "we are going in order by version. not in a rush to launch" and "payments 1.1". |
| 2026-10-20 | v0.5 review ends after one final, severity-rated Codex round (68): release unless it finds a HIGH issue (a way for a hostile site or file to run code, write outside the store, leak a secret, or bypass a permission or owner-only control); medium and low findings are carried to v0.6 as known issues. | Travis: no long review loops. Rounds 58 to 67 had already closed every blocking finding the reviews raised. |
| 2026-10-20 | **v0.5.1 is the next version, before v0.6.** It takes the findings of the 2026-10-20 structure review (section 10, v0.5.1 entry) and nothing else. Permission slips are re-scoped: a slip is a promise from a PERSON, so `slip grant` and `slip revoke` must see a terminal and a typed confirmation; an agent with a shell is otherwise trusted like the owner, and the spec says so. | Travis asked for the review's findings to be the next part to complete. Sixty-eight rounds fixed bugs; the shape (a 1,633-line engine, a reader behind the field, a shield of eight English regexes) was never reviewed, and shape is what contributors meet first at launch. The slip gate closes the one finding that defeats a shipped promise (W-05). |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 item 2 builder's choices. (1) The policy wait pauses the request timer rather than getting a timer of its own: the 15 s then measures only the site (DNS, connect, answer, body), and the wait is bounded by the call's 60 s deadline (a Crawl-delay is at most 30 s). (2) Signing on plain http is allowed only in test mode for the loopback test server (`127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `[::1]`), because the signing tests have no https server; production never signs http. (3) The port check is skipped in test mode (local test servers use random ports); outside it, it applies to every hop, robots.txt and probes included, before anything is sent. (4) Refusal codes for `port_blocked` and `downgrade` are the first word of the fixed message (as `page_too_complex` already was); the port or host goes in `untrusted_content.detail`. (5) A bad `TIRESIAS_ALLOW_PORTS` raises a `ConfigError` naming the variable. | Smallest change that makes each fix testable without the real network; the codes match the existing message convention. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 W-05 builder's choices. (1) The person types the slip's SITE back for grant and for `revoke <id>` (the id when the slip file is damaged, since its site cannot be trusted), and the fixed words `revoke all` for `--all`. (2) There is NO environment or test bypass for the terminal check: any variable a test could set an agent could set too; the tests use a real pseudo-terminal. (3) "Tamper-evident" uses: a deleted use cannot be detected from the use files alone, so uses are counted up to the highest use number present and claims go upward; deleting every use file of a slip still refunds them (a process running as the owner, the stated threat model), and the history line of each claim stays as the record. (4) A revoked slip copied back is counted `damaged` (no new count in the `slips` reply), so `slip list` shows it and `slip revoke --all` removes it. (5) Slips retired by cleanup (expired, used up) also go on the revocation list, since their use files are deleted with them. (6) Touch ID and the Keychain are not built in v0.5.1 (optional in the scope). | The narrowest gate that an agent cannot pass by piping an answer, without a bypass that would undo it; counting by the highest use is the cheapest rule under which a deleted use file is worth nothing. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 N-06 builder's choices. (1) "One request at a time" is one START at a time: requests to a site begin in order, at least the spacing apart; a slow answer does not hold the next request beyond that (holding the turn to the end of the body would need the whole read to run inside the queue, and the spacing already makes overlap rare). (2) The spacing is per origin (scheme, host and port), like Crawl-delay before it. (3) Cross-process spacing uses a lock file rather than the append-only pattern of the cooldown log, because a slot must be read and moved forward in one step; a lock older than 5 s is broken, and any store trouble falls back to the process's own spacing (a request never fails over it). (4) Private calls and form previews never write the spacing file (a private visit must leave no record of which site it called). (5) A first read of a new site therefore takes about 7 seconds (robots.txt, six probes, the page); the probes' answers are cached for an hour, so later reads of the site are not slowed. (6) The test suite turns the spacing off with `TIRESIAS_HOST_SPACING_MS`, honored only under Node's test runner. | The cheapest design that makes Tiresias polite by default across the CLI and MCP server at once, and keeps a private visit traceless. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 PDF child permissions: `--allow-worker` is kept, against the scope's "no workers", because the child's parent watchdog (`parentwatch.ts`) is a worker thread and is what kills a child stuck in a busy loop when Tiresias dies; pdf.js itself runs without a worker in Node. Node 22's permission model does not restrict the network, so that part of the review's finding ("can use the network") stays open: the child is given no address and no fetching code. Reads are allowed in `node_modules` as a whole (where pdfjs-dist and the reader's own dependencies are installed), not pdfjs-dist alone. | The watchdog is a stronger guarantee than removing workers; the store and the owner's files are what the cage must protect. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 records: the receipts field `kind` (quote, form, file) is the record's kind, as the scope says; the file-warning record's file kind, which used that name since v0.5.0, is now `file_kind` (the name history already used). Old lines keep their markers (`sent: "form"`, `file: "warning"`), which readers check first. | One name per meaning across history and receipts; a reader of old lines is never misled because the markers decide. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 shield builder's choices: (1) the cap stays 30, now per type. (2) Only simple `.class` and `#id` selectors of the page's own `<style>` are read; external stylesheets and complex selectors stay a known gap (no CSS engine). (3) Screen-reader-only text counts as hidden: it is real accessibility text on honest sites, but a person looking at the page does not see it, and that is the shield's test. (4) Hidden PDF text is flagged, not removed, except what pdf.js already leaves out (off the page): a tiny footnote or a white watermark can be quoted honestly, and the flag tells the agent it is there. | Proportionate: closes the cheap tricks the review named without a CSS or PDF rendering engine. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 walls, builder's reading of "the first 4,000 visible characters and every form": the test is now made around each marker, not over the page: little text before it (the top of the page) and no real content after it (a `<main>` or `<article>` of 1,200 visible characters). Without the second half, every forum or shop page with a sign-in box in its header would stop as a wall; without the first, any captcha'd comment form would. Padding after the form in plain blocks no longer hides a login page. | A wall stops the agent and hands off to a person, so false walls are costly too; this rule is the narrowest that closes the padding gap the review found. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 JSON diffs: one block per line up to 5,000 lines, then content-defined runs of lines. The diff gives up past 5,000 blocks a side, so one block per line for a 5 MB JSON body would turn every change into "too much changed"; content-defined runs keep both the block count and the locality of a change. | The scope asks for line diffs; this keeps them for every ordinary API answer and degrades gracefully for huge ones. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 W-22 builder's choices: list markers, table pipes and code fences are added by Tiresias to the extracted text (quotes are exact substrings of that text, as before); boilerplate class words match whole words only (`shareholder` is kept); a page with no clear main part is read whole, so the scoring can only remove text from pages where one part plainly dominates. | Readability-style results without a dependency, and a conservative failure mode (read everything) when the scoring is unsure. |
| 2026-10-21 | v0.5.1 CLI: `payment_required` exits 2 like the other deliberate stops (the scope lists wall, flagged, refused and file_warning; a price tag is the same kind of outcome: Tiresias stopped on purpose, nothing failed). `--private` is accepted by every command, as before (commands that write refuse in private mode with their own message). | One rule a script can rely on: 0 did it, 1 broke, 2 stopped on purpose. |

| 2026-10-22 | v0.5.1 walls (Codex round 73): when Tiresias cannot tell for sure whether a page's CSS hides something, it errs toward a wall: CSS can remove TEXT from the count, never a password box or a CAPTCHA. A login box hidden in a modal on a thin page with no `<main>` or `<article>` content is therefore a wall. Text hidden by means a reader without a rendering engine cannot see (external style sheets, scripts) stays out of reach until v0.7. | A false wall costs a person one click; a missed wall breaks principle 3. |
| 2026-10-23 | v0.5.1 walls (Codex round 75): the wall check stops judging CSS. It counts text only where no style could plausibly hide it (an allow-list of safe properties), so any page CSS that might hide text, and any inline style beyond spacing, borders and fonts, makes that text not count. Pages with a login box or CAPTCHA and heavily styled inline content will more often be walls. | The same finding (padding hidden from the count) came back in seven straight rounds after each fix; an allow-list ends the class instead of patching instances, and its failure mode is a false wall (a person's click), never a missed one. |
| 2026-10-23 | **Walls and hidden-text flags are a courtesy and an advisory, not a security boundary (coordinator, after Codex round 76).** A site that disguises its own wall only changes how its own page is reported to the agent; Tiresias still never logs in, never solves anything, and `form_submit` refuses password, CAPTCHA and payment forms on its own. Everything a page says is already returned as `untrusted_content`, so a missed hidden-text flag lowers a hint, not a guarantee. Without a rendering engine, CSS hiding is open-ended, so detection is best effort by design. Codex rounds from 77 are told so: wall evasion by a site disguising its OWN wall, and CSS tricks that avoid the hidden-text flag, are not findings; anything that makes Tiresias send credentials, submit a refused form, run or open content, write outside the store, leak secrets or keys, bypass an owner-only control, or crash or hang is. Round 76's findings 1, 2, 10 and 11 (and the padding part of 5 and 9) fall under this and are noted, not fixed. | The same two classes came back after every fix from round 69 to 76; they protect no boundary the principles promise. v0.7 revisits this decision (W-10, section 7): rendering can see what is actually visible (Travis, 2026-10-23, "log that shift to .7"). |

For each control, its attribute values, every text tied to it (its labels, its references, the labels and legends around it) and the form's context are one bag of words, refused when every word of a many-word marker (card number, security code, one time, pass code and the rest of the PAY, login and CAPTCHA phrases) appears anywhere in it, in any order. Cost: more forms are refused when unrelated words around a field happen to complete a marker (a "Security" heading and a "code" field). |
| 2026-10-23 | v0.5.1 forms (Travis, option a, after Codex round 91): the form guard keeps ONE word set per form. Every text the audit reads (every attribute value of every covered element, each unit's text with text nodes apart and with inline pieces joined) adds its words to it, and the form is refused when every word of any marker is somewhere in it, in any order. One marker list (`MARKER_PHRASES`) holds single words and phrases alike, and each phrase also matches with its words run together in any way ("sign in code", "signin code", "signincode"); "captcha" and "turnstile" match inside a word. The per-control word mix of round 90 is deleted. Each element is visited once, whichever unit reaches it first, so the work is linear; the node budget and 1 MB of text refuse at the limit. **Tradeoff, accepted: more harmless forms are refused.** Words anywhere in the form or tied to it can complete a marker: a contact form with "CC me" and a phone-number field (cc + number), a "security" mention and a ZIP code field (security + code), "Sign up in minutes" and a code field (sign + in + code), "one" and "time" in unrelated sentences. An agent cannot send those; a person still can. | Rounds 87 to 91 each found one more place a field's meaning could live; tying words to fields is open-ended, a whole-form set is not, and its failure is a refusal, never a send. |
| 2026-10-23 | v0.5.1 forms (coordinator, after Codex round 92): visiting an element once may skip duplicate work, never a check. The audit first finds every covered root (one pass over the union of their subtrees, following references), then keeps only the outermost roots by document-order spans recorded in the page walk, and walks each kept root's whole subtree making every check. Kept subtrees are disjoint, so each node of the union is checked exactly once, with its true nesting and its text beside its neighbours; both passes are linear. The merged marker list again holds every phrase of the pre-merge lists ("not robot" restored), enforced by a test that loops over the old lists. The `<pre>` node-count backstop of round 91 is deleted: chooseMain already counts every node the walk reads in a `<pre>` and refuses the page past the limit. | Round 92 showed visit-once skipping checks where a reference reached part of a subtree first; outermost-roots-first removes overlap instead of handling it. |
| 2026-10-23 | v0.5.1 forms (coordinator, after Codex round 93): ONE normalization path for every text the form guard judges: invisible and format characters removed, NFKC, case-folded (upper then lower, so "ß" is "ss"), invisible characters removed again. Every text adds its tokens in three variants: unsplit, camelCase split, and run together within a whitespace-separated chunk ("PassWord", "myPassword", "Pass-word", "P.A.S.S.W.O.R.D" all give "password"). Card-number digits are checked per attribute value and per text node, never on joined strings, after the same normalization (fullwidth digits, invisible characters), allowing single spaces or dashes of any kind between digits. A `<pre>` leaves boilerplate text out like the main walk (links kept), and its node-count backstop is restored. | Round 93 showed the camelCase rule erasing markers and joined attribute values hiding card numbers; one path with every variant ends the normalization class instead of adding cases. |

The many-word markers with their words apart are still checked against the form's word set (whole and camelCase-split words). Each attribute value and each block of inline-joined text is normalized (invisible characters out, NFKC, case fold) and stripped of everything but letters and digits; every marker whose run-together form has 5 characters or more (`SHORT_MARKER`, pinned by a test) is searched inside it, so spacing, punctuation and capitals cannot hide one ("SeCuRiTy Code", "S.e.c.u.r.i.t.y Code", "mySeCuRiTy Code", "ArE YoU HuMaN"). Shorter markers (cvv, otp, csc, iban, 2fa...) are searched only inside one whitespace-separated chunk, so "top tip" is not "otp". The many-word markers with words apart count a word of 5 letters or more found inside any skeleton, a shorter one only as a whole word (whole or camelCase split). Card digits are also checked on each block of inline-joined text. **Cost grows (same direction):** besides the 2026-10-24 list, a long marker now matches across spaces inside one text: "I bypass wording" (password), "Someone timed it" (one time), "Card nothing" (card no), "spin code" (pin code). Pinned by the same test. | Rounds 93 to 95 kept finding one more spelling of a long marker; deleting everything but letters and digits ends spelling as a variable, and the 5-character floor keeps short markers from matching across words. |

A wait is never forgotten or shortened; a collision only makes Tiresias wait longer than asked. The decision to append a line to `cooldowns.jsonl` uses exact sources only. | Hardening rounds 6c and 7c and client-trust round 1 found the same growth three times: dropping expired waits was not enough, and dropping live ones broke principle 2. A fixed-size structure that can only over-estimate removes the class: memory is bounded whatever the number of hostile sites, and every error is in the direction of obeying the site. |

Fixed with tests: a plain copy is never reused for an explicit render, a 304 never stands in for a render, a stale rendered copy is rendered again, and `check` (with or without `--fresh`) of a rendered copy never reuses a rendered parse by the plain HTML's fingerprint. | The same freshness rule as every other read, which the site set itself; an owner or agent who needs a new rendering has `fresh`. Rendering again on every explicit render would multiply browser runs for no new information inside the site's own freshness window. |
| 2026-10-27 | **The key directory is signed live, reversing the 2026-10-16 "pre-signed on this machine" row for Raibit's own copy (Travis, chat; recorded here 2026-11-01).** A Cloudflare Worker on raibit.ai holds a copy of the signing key as an encrypted secret (`TIRESIAS_SIGNING_KEY`) and signs each directory response with a 5-minute expiry. `tiresias keys sign-directory` stays, for owners who host a static directory for a verifier that accepts day-long signatures; README describes both ways and their costs. | Cloudflare's BotBase review rejected the 30-day pre-signed directory as stale and asked for signatures that expire in 60 to 3,600 seconds, renewed on a schedule; a Mac on a timer would lapse whenever it sleeps. The cost is a second copy of the key: if the Cloudflare account were broken into, make a new key (`keys init --force`) and publish the new directory. |
| 2026-10-27 | **W-09's BotBase access type is Intermediary, not Direct (recorded here 2026-11-01; supersedes the 2026-10-17 row).** | Cloudflare's reviewer: Tiresias fetches at an individual person's request and returns the content to that person. Banks had picked Direct at submission. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Row 7.7 designed away: on a rendered page any background image or gradient counts as covering the text under it (Travis, option (a)).** The inspection does not read pixels, so text under a transparent decorative layer can be flagged `covered`: a false flag on an honest page, never hidden text shown. Stated in README and `docs/v0.7/walls.md`; pinned by a rendered test. | Two attempts in the v0.9.1 sweep to judge gradients and images each opened a way to hide text (sweep rounds 1 and 2); a rule that can only over-flag is the safe one for v1.0. Option (b), treating such layers as undecidable (inspection partial), stays available if over-flagging shows up in real use. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Row 7.22 fixed: text with `pointer-events: none` that a hit test cannot find makes the rendered hidden-text inspection partial (Travis, option (c) with (a)'s wording).** Such text cannot be measured for clipping: it is not flagged (that would flag honest click-through text), `hidden_text` is `"partial"` so the HTML hiding checks also run, and the text does not count as proven visible text for wall detection: its characters are counted apart, a visible marker still proves a wall when the page would be thin even with all of them visible, only a page in between is undecided, and any HTML wall fallback on a rendered page honours the browser's hidden marks (the second attempt, after Codex sweep round 4). Markers keep their rule. README and `docs/v0.7/walls.md` state the limit (017f0d6, fe1a96b). | Honest about the gap, cheap, and no false flags; the cost is more `partial` replies on pages that use `pointer-events: none` on text. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Google Web Risk lookups are exempt from site spacing (Travis, confirming the v0.6 settings-track decision; closes the question open since v0.6).** A Web Risk lookup is an API call to Google, not a visit to the site being read, so only `never_visit` and `hand_off` apply to it; `spacing_seconds` and the default spacing do not. | Spacing protects the site being read; Google's own quota governs its API. |
| 2026-11-01 | **W-09 verified: Cloudflare recognises Tiresias's signed requests (Banks).** A signed read of `https://crawltest.com/cdn-cgi/web-bot-auth` answered "this is a verified web-bot-auth-request" (BotBase submission, access type Intermediary). This meets v1.0's "verified on Cloudflare". The Intermediary question in Open questions is answered for Raibit's copy. | Checked once, by hand, per the bot-wall rule (one try). |
| 2026-11-01 | **The user agent keeps `+https://raibit.ai/tiresias/`, and the raibit.ai page stays: on launch day it gains a prominent link to the public repo (Travis; replaces launch prep's plan to make the page a redirect and switch the string to GitHub in v1.0.1, and refines the 2026-10-16 row).** | Travis, 2026-11-01: "keep the page with the link. raibit page building is good." A page on Raibit's own domain builds Raibit, and it is the page Cloudflare's BotBase reviewers read and the listing's docs link. The string never pointed at GitHub in this build anyway: the repo was private when v1.0.0 was tagged, so a GitHub link would have shown site owners a "not found" page. `package.json`'s `homepage` points at the repo, since npm shows it only after publishing. |
| 2026-11-01 | **The public repo starts from a fresh one-commit history, made by `scripts/export-public.sh` from the release tag; the private repo is renamed and kept (Claude, launch prep; Travis approves on launch day).** The export leaves out `docs/reviews/`, `docs/v1.0/runbook.md` and the script itself, adds `docs/reviews/` to the public `.gitignore`, and stops on a file count that differs from the commit, a local user name, a home path, any of the five secret patterns or a Google API key prefix (binary files searched too), a scan that cannot run, or a key, env or package file; it commits under Raibit's GitHub no-reply address, never this machine's git identity (which carries the local user name). Track records in `docs/v0.*` stay: they hold no local user name or home path, and they are the design record the SPEC points at. | Flipping the existing repo public would publish every old commit, review logs included (2026-10-31 says they stay private); rewriting it in place leaves the old commits reachable by their hashes. A fresh history publishes exactly the release tree. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Two Linux-only failures found by CI on v0.9.1 fixed before the tag (Claude, launch prep; Codex reviewed).** The anchor receipt cursor now also keeps the SHA-256 of every byte before its offset and resumes only when the file still hashes the same there (Codex launch round 1 showed that the last 32 bytes alone could match after a same-length rewrite): Linux reuses an inode number at once, so a receipts file deleted and written again kept its device, inode and a size past the offset, and its new records were skipped (a regression test reproduces it on macOS by rewriting a file in place). A hardening test used 120-second waits that ended during a 190-second CI run; it uses hour-long waits. | v1.0 supports Linux and carries no known bug (2026-10-31); macOS runs had passed, CI on Ubuntu had not. |
| 2026-11-01 | **W-31, the page for people, leaves Tiresias's version plan and becomes its own separate project (Travis).** v1.2 is no longer planned; after v1.1 Tiresias is the open engine. The people's UI is built on top of Tiresias as a different product, in its own repo. | Travis: "Its almost a completely different project... the things we focus on for the back end will probably be completely different from what we are working on in the front end." The engine stays free and open; a UI built on it has its own goals, users and release rhythm. |
| 2026-11-01 | **New v1.2 is a security deep dive and v1.3 a compliance deep dive (Travis).** They replace the v1.2 that W-31 held before it moved out to its own project. Scope for each is still to be written. | Travis: "so new 1.2 is security deep dive. 1.3 compliance deep dive for tiersias". |
| 2026-11-01 | **Payments move to v1.3, after security (v1.1) and compliance (v1.2) (Travis, accepting Banks's suggestion).** Supersedes the same-day row that put security at v1.2 and compliance at v1.3, and the 2026-10-20 "payments 1.1". | Payments is the riskiest code, since it moves real money, so it is built on a hardened base; money also has its own legal rules, which the compliance pass checks first. |
| 2026-11-01 | **v1.1 scope approved (Travis): no paid outside audit for now (revisit before v1.3 payments); releases move to GitHub Actions with npm trusted publishing and provenance; Colima installed to test the Docker box; a public v1.1 security review write-up after the fixes ship.** Scope: `docs/v1.1/scope.md`. | A paid audit matters most once money moves; provenance lets anyone verify a release came from the public code, with no npm token stored; the Docker doc has never been run; the review is credibility on display. |
| 2026-11-01 | **GET side effects: honest wording (Travis, v1.1 D1).** Permission slips gate sending forms; reading a link is a read, as in every browser. A site that changes something on a plain GET breaks HTTP's rule that GET is safe, and that is on the site (as with echoed secrets, 2026-10-31). SECURITY.md says so. A rendered page's scripts may not request a URL matching a GET form action on that page. | No browser can gate every GET without breaking reading; an allowlist would make Tiresias unusable for ordinary reads. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Private calls and form previews take part in cross-process spacing (Travis, v1.1 D2),** writing only the politeness state it needs (origin and times), like the Retry-After exception. Refines the private-mode rows. | Obeying sites comes first; the note says nothing about the page. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Hooks inherit the owner's environment minus Tiresias's own secrets (Travis, v1.1 D3).** `TIRESIAS_WEB_RISK_KEY` and any other secret-bearing `TIRESIAS_*` variable is removed. Refines the row that let hooks inherit the environment. | Leaking a Tiresias key is HIGH in SECURITY.md, and a hook has no need for it. |
| 2026-11-01 | **Text views mark web content (Travis, v1.1 D4).** `--text` views and the default Markdown `log` and `needs` stay readable, mark where web-derived text begins, and the docs point agents to the JSON output. | People read these views; agents have JSON with `untrusted_content`. |
| 2026-11-01 | **The four v1.1 decisions (D1 to D4) are owner settings whose defaults are Travis's picks (Travis: "save all these choices for settings. I just labeled what it comes out of the box").** `get_form_actions` (`read` default, `slip`), `private_spacing` (`shared` default, `none`), `hook_secrets` (`hidden` default, `inherit`), `text_views` (`marked` default, `wrapped`). Owner-only; `none` and `inherit` loosen a protection and are risky settings under W-32. Details: `docs/v1.1/findings.md`. | Owners differ; the defaults are the safe, sensible choice, and the other side stays available without a code change. |
| 2026-11-06 | **`keys rotate`: the signing key is rotated with an overlap window (v1.1 workstream 4, Claude).** A new Ed25519 key replaces `ed25519.private.jwk` (temp file, then rename; 0600 in a 0700 folder; refused in a synced folder and in private mode). Every request from then on is signed with the new key only. The old key stays in the published directory (`keys show`, `keys directory`, `keys sign-directory`) for the overlap, 7 days by default, `--overlap` 0 to 30, and then drops out by itself; `keys rotate --finish` drops it at once. A second rotation inside the window is refused until `--finish`, so no key is lost without the owner saying so. `keys init --force` also drops a previous key (a fresh start). Rotations are written to history (tool `keys`) and listed in the daily log with the risky settings. | Today a leaked or old key could only be replaced by `keys init --force`, which cuts off every site that cached the old directory at once. An overlap lets verifiers pick up the new directory first. Thirty days is the cap because a longer window keeps an old key trusted longer than any reason to rotate it would allow. |
| 2026-11-06 | **During the overlap the old PRIVATE key is kept, in `keys/ed25519.previous.jwk`, for one job only: signing the directory response (Claude).** Cloudflare asks for one signature per key listed in the directory (row 2026-10-16), so a directory that lists the old key needs the old key's signature too. The file has the signing key's storage rules, is never used to sign a request (`signing.ts` reads only the current key), and is deleted when the window ends (by the next keys command that looks) or on `--finish`. `keys sign-directory` during the window signs with both keys (`sig1` new, `sig2` old) and caps the signatures' expiry at the window's end, so a static copy never keeps publishing the old key past it; the reminder says to sign again after that time. After a leak, run `keys rotate --finish` first if an overlap is open. Then `keys rotate --overlap 0` keeps nothing: the old key is deleted and out of the directory at once. | The alternative, deleting the old private key at once and signing the directory with the new key only, publishes a key with no signature, which Cloudflare's rule does not allow, so the directory could fail verification for the whole window. Keeping the old key for the directory alone, for days, in the same owner-only folder it already lived in, adds no new exposure; the leak case has its own zero-overlap path. |
| 2026-11-06 | **Compute-day sweep of Codex K.1 to K.7 on `keys rotate` (Claude).** Signing-key rotations, `--finish` and any cleanup of the previous key hold a lock (`keys/ed25519.rotate.lock`, the saved-page lock style) and look again before deleting, so a `keys show` that read the key just before a rotation never deletes the kept old key. Leftover previous-key temp files (a rotation killed mid-write) are swept by rotate, `--finish` and window expiry. Slip key rotations hold their own lock (`keys/slips.rotate.lock`), make every refusal (synced folder, another rotation, a full list) BEFORE revoking any slip, and recheck that the key being retired is still the current one. Every retired slip public half is kept (cap 100,000, past which rotation is refused, never a silent drop). | Codex r1 on f21d6a3 (`docs/reviews/2026-11-06-v11-keys-rotate-codex-r1.md`). |
| 2026-11-06 | **`keys rotate --slips` rotates the slip key and revokes every permission slip (Claude).** It needs the exact phrase `--confirm "YES, ROTATE THE SLIP KEY"` always, even with confirmations for risky settings turned off (it is an irreversible action, not a setting), and a person at a terminal who types `rotate slip key` back, like `slip revoke --all`, so an agent cannot use it to revoke slips. Every slip is revoked first, on the record in `slips/revoked.jsonl`, then the key is replaced, then any slip granted meanwhile is revoked too. The old key's PUBLIC half is kept in `keys/slips.retired.json`, so `saved verify` still checks snapshots signed before the rotation: they report `signature: "retired_key"` with the date and a note that anyone holding a leaked old key could also have signed them. A retired key never checks a slip. A slip key that cannot be read (damaged, readable by others) is replaced all the same without a retired entry. Receipts never reference the slip key (a receipt names its snapshot by SHA-256), and anchored proofs do not depend on it. Written to history (tool `keys`, with the number of slips revoked). | A slip signed by the old key would fail its signature check anyway and count as damaged; revoking them all first makes that a clean, recorded end instead of a pile of damaged slips. Snapshots are evidence the owner keeps, so a rotation should not make them unverifiable; keeping only the public half keeps them checkable without keeping the secret. |
| 2026-11-10 | **The form promise is the permission slip; recognizing sign-in, payment and purchase forms is best-effort (Travis).** SECURITY.md leads with "No form is sent without your permission slip". On top of the slip, every form recognized as a sign-in, password, CAPTCHA, payment or purchase form is refused, slip or not. A password field, a card field marked as one and an embedded CAPTCHA frame are recognized from the markup; a form that only says what it does in words is recognized from word lists in 13 languages. The earlier wording ("never sent, slip or not") is replaced. | Two review rounds in a row found labels the word lists missed (section 10, v1.1.0: R.2, R.3, R.8, R.9), and no list can recognize every wording. The promise now states what the code can keep. |
| 2026-11-10 | **`get_form_actions=slip` gates other spellings of a known GET form action, not other addresses a server sends to the same handler (Claude, sweep finding S.1; Codex agreed in the confirming round).** The comparison folds encoding, case, dot segments and punctuation. `/vote.json` where the form said `/vote` is not gated, and SECURITY.md says so. | Only the server knows its routes, so that class has no end. The same comparison is always on for a rendered page's script requests: folding extensions would make a form to `/search` block the page's own `/search.js`, and prefix matching would block `/search/suggest`. The setting is an opt-in tightening, and sending a form always needs a slip. |

### Open questions

- Will Cloudflare register other owners' self-run copies as Intermediaries? Raibit's copy was accepted as an Intermediary and is verified (2026-10-27, 2026-11-01); each owner signs with their own key and registers their own copy.
- How does a brand-new, unknown signer earn trust from sites?
- Package name: `@raibitsi/tiresias` (plain `tiresias` is taken on npm); the `raibitsi` npm organization (Travis made it on 2026-11-01; `raibit` was already taken on npm).
- Launch-day items from the v0.9 hardening track (`docs/v0.9/hardening.md`, "For Travis" items 1 and 3) are steps in `docs/v1.0/runbook.md`: turn on GitHub's private vulnerability reporting, and pin the CI actions by commit (needs a `workflow`-scope login). Still open: whether pages of 8 KB or less keep being parsed on the main thread without a time budget (item 7; recommendation: keep, with the fuzz script's `slow_inline` check as the tripwire).

---

## 10. Version history

Newest first. Each entry lists what was added, changed, removed, and the wants and needs it closed.

### v1.1.0 (2026-11-10)

The security deep dive (Travis, 2026-11-01; scope `docs/v1.1/scope.md`). Every promise in SECURITY.md was tested end to end, the way a hostile page, file, server or fooled agent would, and each finding was fixed with a test or stated as a limit in SECURITY.md. The full list and its triage: `docs/v1.1/findings.md`.

- **Audit (Codex, nine defensive audits, one per promise plus a fully prompt-injected agent):** 21 distinct items, each fixed with a regression test (Codex wrote, Claude reviewed). Web text in `--text` views and the default `log` and `needs` output is marked; the PDF child may not start worker threads; calendars always parse in the worker; the Web Risk request gets the private-address and port checks; a cross-origin robots.txt redirect is followed only to that origin's own `/robots.txt`; Retry-After on a redirect is obeyed before following; a Crawl-delay past the wait budget declines the read, and merged groups keep the longest delay; an active Retry-After survives any growth of the cooldown log and fails closed when unreadable; purchase forms without card fields and sign-in forms without a password field are recognized and refused; a slip is rechecked right before sending; echoed secrets are looked for in full Office text and PDF titles before shortening; outline building is bounded (`page_too_complex`); waiting rendered requests keep bounded header fields; history and receipt scans stop on cancellation or at 60 seconds; agent-chosen bookmark names stay inside `untrusted_content`; MCP `resume` text is recorded as `provided_by_agent`.
- **Owner settings (Travis's four decisions, section 9, 2026-11-01):** `get_form_actions` (`read`, or `slip`), `private_spacing` (`shared`, or `none` after confirmation), `hook_secrets` (`hidden`, or `inherit` after confirmation), `text_views` (`marked`, or `wrapped`).
- **Keys:** `keys rotate` swaps the signing key with a 7-day overlap (`--overlap` 0 to 30, `--finish`); `--slips` makes a new slip key and revokes every slip (section 9, 2026-11-06). Review findings K.1 to K.9 fixed: rotations hold a lock that only a provably dead owner can lose.
- **Machine translations are scanned by the shield (Travis, 2026-11-09, finding P.1):** an instruction written in a language the shield does not check is now caught in its translation; flags from a translation stay with the reply.
- **Attack lab:** a local site of hostile pages on 127.0.0.1, run in the suite (hidden instructions, fake walls, private-address redirects, robots and delay abuse, compression and Office bombs, booby-trapped PDFs, hostile rendered pages, hostile JSON, pay and sign-in forms). Its first review found tests too weak to prove their claims (L.1 to L.6, M.1 to M.8); all were tightened.
- **Fuzzing:** two new targets, `mcp` (malformed tool calls into the real MCP server) and `formflow` (hostile forms through preview and submit). Hour-long runs: 156,980 inputs through the 20 parser targets and 131,040 through the new ones, with no crash, hang or form sent without a slip.
- **Final review of the whole branch (Codex, five rounds):** R.1 to R.12. An encoded spelling of a known GET form action skipped the `slip` setting's check, so the comparison is now one fold, repeated until stable, that keeps only letters and digits per path segment; the purchase and sign-in word lists grew from English to 13 languages; a private read enforces every gate ordinary reads have learned; `keys sign-directory` cannot mix key generations. The form promise was restated (section 9, 2026-11-10).
- **Sweep (2026-11-10):** a navigation refused before the page's load event no longer makes the render wait its full limit, and a render cannot settle while a paused request is still being checked (N.2, S.2). S.1 is a stated limit (section 9, 2026-11-10). Open, test housekeeping only: tests and the fuzzer leave their temp folders behind (N.3).
- **Releases:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` publishes a version tag through npm trusted publishing: the tag must match `package.json`, the suite runs, a CycloneDX SBOM is attached to the GitHub release, and the package is staged with provenance until the owner approves it. No npm token is stored anywhere.
- **Code scanning:** GitHub CodeQL's 44 alerts on the public repo were read and triaged at release: 42 are in tests and the fuzzer's self-test, one is the stated store-swap limit, and one is the ordinary request path sending stored validators and addresses, by design (`docs/v1.1/findings.md`; Codex checked both).
- **Version:** 1.1.0; `PARSER_VERSION` 16, so cached pages are re-read once and their form listings use the new recognition.
- **Tests:** 1190 of 1190, 0 skipped (macOS, with Chrome).

### v1.0.1 (2026-11-01)

The first bug-clearing release after launch (Travis's zero-bug rule, section 9, 2026-10-31). No new features.

- **Hunt:** six parallel read-only Codex audits of all source, one per area (network, reading, rendering, files, forms and store, interfaces), `reviews/2026-11-01-v101-audit-*` (private). HIGH 0, MEDIUM 8, LOW 9.
- **Fixed, each with a regression test (Codex wrote, Claude reviewed):** cancelled waits no longer leave future spacing reservations; an unsolicited robots.txt 304 is retried once, then the site is treated as saying no; malformed Retry-After values take the fallback cooldown; links follow the page's first `<base href>` (http or https only) while receipts keep the fetched URL; the rendered inspection judges every text rectangle, not the first fifty, and unreachable points are still skipped as row 7.22 decided; block splits never cut a surrogate pair; plain-format reads report text omitted at the expansion cap; a fresh cached copy honors the current rendering setting and the call's render request; markdown twins keep the query string; Office replies never keep text whose receipts were dropped; RAR, gzip and other non-ZIP signatures are not renamed into Office or programs by a filename; concurrent saves share a lock with cleanup, so the cap holds and nothing older is evicted; restored confirmed site restrictions no longer reject a valid config; a call keeps the settings it started with (raw config, effective values and confirmations snapshotted per call); `settings` in private mode writes nothing; an unrelated bad storage limit no longer blocks resume; history keeps questions to the 1,000-character purpose bound and marks cuts.
- **Review finds fixed before release (Claude):** Codex's first lock for saves was the never-broken config lock, which a killed MCP server would leave forever; saves now use `src/savedlock.ts`, an atomic lock directory that only a dead owner (ESRCH) can lose. Codex's first inspection fix also marked unreachable points partial, against row 7.22; reverted to skipping them.
- **Version:** 1.0.1; `PARSER_VERSION` 15, so cached pages are re-read once.
- **Tests:** 1041 of 1041, 0 skipped (macOS, outside the sandbox).

### v1.0.0 (2026-11-01)

The first public release: launch quality as section 7 defines it (docs, demo, tests, Codex-reviewed, verified on Cloudflare), macOS and Linux. No new features; what v0.1 to v0.9.1 built, made ready for strangers, plus two Linux fixes found by CI. Tagged on private main; making the repo public and publishing to npm wait for Travis (runbook).

- **Verified on Cloudflare (W-09, section 9, 2026-11-01).** Raibit's copy is listed in Cloudflare's bot directory as an Intermediary and its signed requests verify. The key directory on raibit.ai is signed live by a Cloudflare Worker since 2026-10-27 (section 9); README now describes live signing and `keys sign-directory`'s static files as the two ways to host a directory, and the command's help says which it is for.
- **Fixed (section 9, 2026-11-01):** the anchor receipt cursor on Linux (inode reuse; it now checks a digest of everything before its offset; two regression tests) and a hardening test's waits that ended mid-run on CI. "v0.x never pays" in two refusal texts now reads "this version of Tiresias never pays".
- **Docs for strangers:** README (status, install from npm as the launch-day path with from source kept, MCP lines for an npm install, signing, Cloudflare, the translator examples carry their confirmation, no "until v1.0" wording), QUICKSTART, SECURITY.md (supported versions, Windows), CHANGELOG v1.0.0 with a newcomer's summary of v0.1 to v0.9.1, and `docs/DEMO.md`: a walkthrough with real output from this build in a fresh home (a read, a question with receipts, checking receipts, a bot wall handed to a person and resumed from a saved page, Claude Code setup).
- **Public export:** `scripts/export-public.sh` (section 9, 2026-11-01). **Runbook:** `docs/v1.0/runbook.md` (private): Travis's steps and Banks's, in order, with rollback.
- **Version:** 1.0.0 (`package.json`, `src/types.ts`, user agent `Tiresias/1.0.0 (+https://raibit.ai/tiresias/)`); `homepage` is the GitHub repo; Docker tag `tiresias:1.0`; `docs/DEMO.md` ships in the package.
- **Codex:** five read-only rounds over the v1.0 diff and the export script (`reviews/2026-11-01-v10-launch-round-1` to `-5`, private): HIGH 5, MEDIUM 11, LOW 2 in rounds 1 to 4, every one fixed (mostly the export script and runbook; one in the anchor fix itself); round 5 clean. Full suite twice: 1017 of 1017, 0 skipped; CI green on Ubuntu and macOS.
- **Record:** `docs/v1.0/launch.md` (checklist, decisions, Codex rounds, test counts).

### v0.9.1 (2026-10-31)

The pre-v1.0 sweep of every deferred finding (section 9, 2026-10-31: v1.0 is not tagged while any is open). The 67 MEDIUM and LOW findings deferred from v0.7.0 (23), v0.8.0 (16) and v0.9.0 (28 new, plus 3 repeats) were each fixed with a test that fails without the fix, or designed away; the two that needed Travis were decided on 2026-11-01. Also `tiresias mcp` names held-back settings on standard error (section 9, 2026-10-31), and the two v0.9 rehearsal questions are closed.

- **Who wrote what.** Codex wrote the fixes, in batches by area (anchor and OTS; save page; walls, in two parts; render, CDP and cache; pipeline, translate and resume; confirmations; store, reader, forms and PDF; CLI, MCP, echo and ceiling), in three full clones in parallel (Codex cannot commit inside a git worktree). Claude reviewed each batch (read the diff, checked each fix closes the row and opens nothing new, ran the affected tests, with Chrome), committed it, and built the startup notice. Checklist and log: `docs/v0.9.1-sweep.md`.
- **Claude's review corrections** (small, noted in each commit): the anchor export's rollback keeps the first error; one hidden block is one mark however deep its text (C1's climb stopped at the first ancestor not itself `display: none`; test); a restored confirmed exception is never looser than what the raw file asks (9.5; test); the memory ceiling tracks full-collection and poll readings apart (9.23; test); two test fixtures (a CAPTCHA class the selector never matched, a detail's wording) and one integration fixture (a hand-written rendered cache entry without 7.9's new field).
- **Sent back to Codex after Claude's review (second attempts):** 7.3 (named page elements could also shadow `document`'s own members: `<img name="body">`), 7.10 (the overlay note fired for a fixed background behind the text), 9.11 (the depth cap let text below a hidden root at the cap escape its mark on rendered reads). Each closed on its second attempt. Row 7.7 reached the two-fix stop in the sweep rounds (below).
- **Closed: 67 of 67.** Fixed with a test: 64 (7.22 among them, by Travis's option (c), 017f0d6). ### v0.9.0 (2026-10-31)

Risky settings need an all-caps confirmation (W-32), a hardening audit of the whole codebase, a trusted MCP client with a built-in memory ceiling and optional Docker (W-33), a site's echo of a secret withheld instead of masked, and a private launch rehearsal. Closes W-32 and W-33.

- **Built as four tracks in series on branch `v0.9`** from e0ae6b1 (v0.8.0 plus a README fix, 859 tests): confirmations (track 1, 9f702cd, 873 tests; Codex rounds 1 to 6, 15 HIGH fixed, round 6 clean), hardening (track 2, d3ee1ff, 883 tests; four parallel Codex passes, disk and processes, parsers, the MCP surface and README promises, 18 HIGH fixed or designed away; two HIGH classes, MCP-caller resource exhaustion and echo masking, stopped under the two-fix rule and taken to Travis as items A and B), client trust (track 2b, 293f80f, 891 tests; Travis's answers to A and B; Codex rounds 1 to 3, 2 HIGH fixed, the cooldown-memory class closed by design with the fixed-size store Travis approved) and the rehearsal (track 3, 951822c, 894 tests; Codex round 1: no HIGH, its one MEDIUM fixed with a test). Then reviewed together (below).
- **Added:** `src/confirm.ts` (risky list, phrases, the confirmation record, the in-force clamp, the reply notice), `src/ceiling.ts`, `src/echo.ts`, `src/slots.ts`, `src/nodecheck.ts`; `confirmations.json` in the store; `--confirm` on `settings set|reset`, `scanner set|off`, `translator set|off`; `tiresias settings confirm <name> [<site>]` and `settings confirmations off|on`; `settings_notice` on CLI and MCP replies; `TIRESIAS_CONFIRM`, `TIRESIAS_MEMORY_MB`; `form.reason: "secret_withheld"`; `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `docs/docker.md`, `docs/QUICKSTART.md`, `SECURITY.md`'s threat model; SPDX headers; `scripts/fuzz.mjs` (`npm run fuzz`, opt-in).
- **Changed:** loosening a risky setting needs its phrase, and a hand edit that loosens waits for confirmation (upgrading from v0.8 with saving, rendering or anchoring on: confirm each once). Unknown `Content-Encoding` fails the request; robots.txt that is not text, a 206, or undecodable means the site says no, and every 2xx is followed. CLI `receipts` and `history` print the MCP envelope (`items` is gone). Per-process caps on MCP calls (8, 32 waiting), parser workers (4, 64 waiting) and renders (2, 8 waiting); the MCP message boundary caps arguments and open requests and pauses reading while output backs up. The in-process cooldown list holds 10,000 waits by name and the rest in a fixed 8 MiB keyed store. Form-answer echoes are withheld whole (masking, `[hidden value]` and `counts.receipts_withheld` are gone). Labels Tiresias makes (`(top of page)`, `(structured data)`) get no receipt; `PARSER_VERSION` 12 to 13 (cached pages are read again once). Plain messages for an old Node, a mistyped option, an address without `https://`, an unwritable store and being offline.
- **Decisions:** section 9, the v0.9 rows dated 2026-10-31 (W-32, W-33 and items A and B, the cooldown store, reviews private at launch, Touch ID after v1.0, macOS and Linux for v1.0, and v1.0 not tagged while any deferred finding is open); two rehearsal questions pending Travis in Open questions. Full track records in `docs/v0.9/confirm.md`, `hardening.md`, `client-trust.md` and `rehearsal.md`.

Three rows repeat findings already in the v0.7.0 and v0.8.0 tables (marked "same as"); they are listed here for the track that found them again and counted once.

| Track, round | Severity | Summary | File | Fix or design away | Closed (v0.9.1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| confirm 1 | MEDIUM | Spacing is compared with the largest wildcard above, not the closest one the readers use (largely addressed by round 1's effect-based comparison; to be re-checked) | `src/confirm.ts` | Fix: compare against the closest wildcard, as the readers resolve it, with a test | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 1 | MEDIUM | Twenty later changes push a loosening out of `recent` and end its 24-hour notice early | `src/confirm.ts` | Fix: keep notice-relevant events for their full 24 hours | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 1 | MEDIUM | Some reply paths carry no `settings_notice`: CLI error replies and thrown errors, `profile` JSON, MCP's too-large fallback | `src/cli.ts`, `src/mcp.ts` | Fix: attach the notice on every reply path | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 2 | MEDIUM | Comparisons ignore what is always allowed: a `ports` list of only 443, or resetting a 1-second spacing, asks for a phrase though nothing changes in force | `src/confirm.ts` | Fix: compare effective values (defaults and always-allowed ports included) | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 3 | MEDIUM | With config.json removed or emptied, restoring a confirmed wildcard ban leaves out its confirmed exact-host `false` exception, so that host is blocked though it was allowed (too strict, not too loose) | `src/confirm.ts` | Fix: restore the confirmed exceptions with the ban | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 4 | MEDIUM | While `sites` does not parse, `scanner off` and `translator off` are refused too (they only remove a hook) | `src/confirm.ts` | Fix: allow removals of top-level hooks; keep refusing site changes | 1a62eb4 |
| confirm 1 | LOW | The command a scanner or translator refusal suggests puts `--confirm` after `--`, where it becomes a program argument | `src/scanner.ts`, `src/translate.ts` | Fix: put `--confirm` before `--` | 1a62eb4 |
| hardening 1a | MEDIUM | `writeJson()` ignores `writeSync`'s byte count, so a short write (disk full) publishes a truncated JSON file (caches, tabs, anchor batches) | `src/store.ts` | Fix: loop until every byte is written, refuse zero progress, remove the temp file on failure | 75e6409 |
| hardening 1a | MEDIUM | `receipts prove --out` writes its files one by one; a later collision or failure leaves earlier files behind and a retry then fails | `src/anchor.ts` | Fix: write to exclusive temp names, then link into place without overwriting; remove only this run's files on failure | 65b8e55 |
| hardening 1b | MEDIUM | Deeply nested HTML lists grow indentation quadratically: a 132 KB page gave 52 M characters of text in the worker, then cloned and cached on the main thread | `src/reader.ts` | Fix: cap the indentation depth (8 levels) and the worker's result size (`page_too_complex`) | 75e6409 |
| hardening fuzz | MEDIUM | Deep nesting is super-linear: about 0.5 MB of nested `<table><tr><td>` (parse) or `<svg>` (forms walk) runs past the 5 s budget and ends as `page_too_complex` (the cap holds; such a page cannot be read) | `src/reader.ts`, `src/forms.ts` | Fix: cap the depth tracked (256 levels, deeper content flattened), with a fuzz seed for each | 75e6409, 59fecdd |
| hardening 1b | MEDIUM | Rendered reads inspect only the first 2,000 inputs and CAPTCHA candidates without saying so, so a password box after them gives a definite "no wall" (same as v0.7 walls 1) | `src/inspect.ts` | Fix: count what was not inspected; "not decided" (HTML fallback) when anything was left | 2bf4cb9 (as 7.4) |
| hardening 1b | LOW | A CDP message that crosses the size cap in its final chunk is accepted (same as v0.7 render 1, rated MEDIUM there) | `src/cdp.ts` | Fix: check held length plus the final fragment before joining | faf5038 (as 7.14) |
| hardening 1c | MEDIUM | MCP `history` and `receipts` with an invalid `since` throw a plain SDK error instead of the envelope | `src/mcp.ts` | Fix: catch and return an `error` envelope with fixed text | fe5bf03 |
| hardening 1d | MEDIUM | A `section` that does not exist returns `status: "error"` after `saveNow([])` already saved a snapshot (same as v0.8 integration 1, rated LOW there) | `src/pipeline.ts` | Fix: check the section before saving; save nothing on that branch | f676107 (as 8.13) |
| hardening 1d | MEDIUM | A saved Office read logs its receipts before the fingerprint-only snapshot exists, so those receipts carry no `snapshot` | `src/pipeline.ts` | Fix: create the snapshot first and pass its id into `fileWarning`; or design away by stating in README that fingerprint-only saves link no receipts | f676107 |
| hardening 1d | MEDIUM | Anchor calendar answers' Retry-After (429/503) is not recorded, and an existing cooldown for a calendar is not checked | `src/anchor.ts` | Fix: check `cooldownLeft` before asking and `setCooldown` on 429/503 (calendars stay outside robots.txt by design) | 65b8e55 |
| hardening 1d | LOW | With signing set up, a plain-http read never loads the key, so a damaged key file goes unnoticed on http-only use | `src/safety.ts` | Design away: README says http is never signed; optionally check the key once per call when signing is configured | designed away (section 9, 2026-10-31); fe5bf03 |
| hardening 2d | MEDIUM | Fenced code in a markdown page loses its indentation and line breaks; HTML `<pre>` is kept | `src/reader.ts` | Fix: send fenced and indented markdown code through the `<pre>` path (README states the gap meanwhile) | 75e6409 |
| hardening 2d | MEDIUM | `saved verify` and `receipts verify` print a failed verification as pretty JSON with `status: "ok"` and exit 1, unlike every other error | `src/cli.ts` | Fix: a failed verification is `status: "error"` (or its own `failed` status) through the normal error printer | fe5bf03 |
| hardening 6c | MEDIUM | The PDF child's slot is released before the next waiter takes it, so a third child can start in between (each child keeps its own memory and time caps) | `src/pdfproc.ts` | Fix: use `Slots` (`src/slots.ts`), which hands the slot over directly | 75e6409 |
| hardening 1c | LOW | Tool annotations: `read` says read-only though `save` writes a snapshot; `bookmark`, `resume`, `tab` say not destructive though they can delete or consume | `src/mcp.ts`, `src/browsetools.ts` | Fix: `readOnlyHint: false` on `read`, `destructiveHint: true` on the three | fe5bf03 |
| client trust 2 | MEDIUM | The ceiling's garbage-collection callback reads the heap when it runs (a later turn), not at the end of the collection, so allocations in between count; short-lived use could stop the server | `src/ceiling.ts` | Fix: stop only on two readings in a row over the ceiling (or old space alone over); document when the reading is taken | fe5bf03 |
| client trust 1 | MEDIUM | A secret of 6 or more characters whose canonical form is shorter than 6 (quotes, backslashes, `+` removed) loses its canonical check, so an encoded spelling is caught only if it also appears plainly | `src/echo.ts` (`secretsOf`) | Fix: apply the 6-character rule to the original value only; keep any non-empty canonical form | fe5bf03 |
| client trust 1 | MEDIUM | Numeric character references with leading zeroes past 6 hex or 7 decimal digits, and named entities outside the matcher's table, are not decoded though the HTML reader decodes them; the post-parse check sees previews, so a long secret's prefix could pass | `src/echo.ts`, `src/formflow.ts` | Fix: allow leading zeroes, use the reader's own entity table, run the post-parse check on the full extracted text | fe5bf03 |
| rehearsal | LOW | A plain read of a JavaScript-only page comes back `ok` with an empty outline and no word that it needs rendering | `src/pipeline.ts` | Fix: a fixed sentence when the HTML `looksLikeShell` and rendering was not asked | 3f99ef1 |
| rehearsal | LOW | `read --save` on an unchanged page says "Answered from the local cache" while `cache` says `refetched` | `src/pipeline.ts` | Fix: "Unchanged since ...; fetched again to save it" | 3f99ef1 |
| rehearsal | LOW | `history --text` and `receipts --text` start with the page-read header `OK    (door: none)` | `src/cli.ts` | Fix: a listing gets its own first line ("History: N records, page P of Q") | fe5bf03 |
| rehearsal | LOW | With a home folder that cannot be written, every command downloads the 11 MB phishing list again and prints four lines to standard error | `src/safebrowsing.ts` | Design away: when the store root cannot be created, skip the download and say `safety: unchecked` once | designed away (section 9, 2026-10-31); 1a62eb4 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | An unsolicited 304 bypasses the parser-version re-read: with an older cached parse Tiresias sends no validators, but a server that answers 304 anyway gets the old parse served (and the label receipts the rehearsal removed come back) | `src/pipeline.ts` (304 branch) | Fix: refuse a 304 when the cached parse is from an older parser, and accept revalidation only when matching validators were sent | 3f99ef1 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | The JSON reader's `(JSON)` heading is not marked generated, so a JSON read quotes `(JSON)` in a receipt (which can be saved and anchored), and `saved verify` accepts generated headings as source text | `src/reader.ts`, `src/saved.ts` | Fix: mark the heading generated, exclude generated headings from verification's source texts, test JSON and legacy label receipts | 3f99ef1 |

### v0.8.0 (2026-10-31)

Save page (W-28) and tamper-proof receipts (W-08). With the owner's leave (`saving`, off by default), a read that asks keeps a signed snapshot of the exact bytes it received, and every receipt of that read names it; `tiresias saved` lists, shows, verifies and deletes them. With the owner's leave (`anchoring`, off by default), `tiresias anchor` timestamps the fingerprints of receipt records and snapshots through OpenTimestamps (free, Bitcoin-backed): one salted Merkle root per batch is all that leaves the machine; `receipts prove` exports a standard `.ots` proof and `receipts verify` checks one offline without ever calling it proven. Closes W-08 and W-28.

- **Built as two tracks in series on branch `v0.8`** from 11588eb (v0.7.0, 837 tests): save page (track 1, 6c353b5, 847 tests; Codex rounds 1 to 4, 8 HIGH fixed, all in the store's fail-closed checks, the cap and quote replay; round 4 clean) and tamper-proof receipts (track 2, 4cacb3b, 855 tests; Codex rounds 1 and 2, 2 HIGH fixed: offline verify never says proven, and only attestations below the claimed root count; round 2 clean). Then reviewed together (`docs/reviews/2026-10-31-v08-integration-round-*.md`, below).
- **Added:** `src/saved.ts`, `src/anchor.ts`, `src/ots.ts`; `read` option `save` (MCP and CLI `--save`); top-level `saved`; receipts' `snapshot`; the receipts listing's `record_fingerprint`; `config.json` keys `saving`, `max_saved_mb`, `keep_saved_days`, `anchoring`, `anchor_calendars`; `tiresias saved [list|show|verify|delete]`, `tiresias anchor [status|upgrade]`, `tiresias receipts prove|verify`; history's `saved`; the store's `saved/` and `anchors/` folders.
- **Changed:** `safeFetch` can hand back the body as received (`keepRaw`), before charset decoding. `clear` and the daily trim handle snapshots (`keep_saved_days`, leftovers, a lowered `max_saved_mb`); neither `saved/` nor `anchors/` counts toward `max_disk_mb`. The anchor track's `fingerprint` field on receipts-listing items was renamed `record_fingerprint` at integration, so a file-warning record's own `fingerprint` is unchanged (test added).
- **Decisions:** section 9, the v0.8 rows dated 2026-10-31, and W-32 (risky settings need confirmation, v0.9); full track records in `docs/v0.8/save.md` and `docs/v0.8/anchor.md`.

#### Deferred to compute-day sweep

Deferred by Travis's rule (fix HIGH only mid-week; MEDIUM and LOW go to the Thursday compute-day sweep). The anchor rows go first: a hostile calendar can leave a batch unexportable or damaged while its receipts count as anchored, so those receipts lose their proof until the fix.

| Track, round | Severity | Summary | File | Note | Closed (v0.9.1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| anchor 1 | MEDIUM | A calendar answer may use the full nesting depth (256); the exported .ots then prefixes the Merkle path and cannot be written, so one hostile calendar can stop every export from a batch another calendar served well. Reserve depth for the path when accepting answers, use the official bound (under 256), and drop a bad branch while keeping good ones. | `src/anchor.ts`, `src/ots.ts` | **First on Thursday** (a batch can lose its proof) | 65b8e55 |
| anchor 1 | MEDIUM | Bitcoin attestation heights are not range-checked; the official reader refuses heights over 2^32-1. Refuse them when parsing. | `src/ots.ts` |  | 65b8e55 |
| anchor 2 | MEDIUM | Merged proofs are checked only for size, not for the parser's 20,000-node limit; several answers or upgrades together can pass it, and the batch then reads as damaged (upgrade and prove refuse it) while its fingerprints count as anchored. Check the node count on every merge (with room for the Merkle path) and keep the previous proof when an addition would pass it. | `src/anchor.ts`, `src/ots.ts` | **First on Thursday** (a batch can lose its proof) | 65b8e55 |
| anchor 2 | MEDIUM | Litecoin and Ethereum attestations are kept as opaque payloads without checking them; the official reader parses those tags, so a malformed one from a hostile calendar makes the whole exported .ots unreadable to it. Check their payloads, or refuse those tags before merging. | `src/ots.ts` |  | 65b8e55 |
| save 1 | MEDIUM | The manifest keeps only the first 1,000 characters of Content-Type; a charset parameter past that is lost, so verify decodes intact bytes differently and reports `not_found`. Store the decoder the read used (or the whole bounded header). | `src/saved.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 1 | MEDIUM | `saveSnapshot` does not enforce `MAX_PART` (16 MB), which verify enforces; with a raised `TIRESIAS_MAX_BYTES` a snapshot can be written that never verifies. Refuse such a save before writing (a fixed result). | `src/saved.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 2 | MEDIUM | Quote matching joins the texts with NUL and rejects quotes holding NUL, but a text page can contain NUL after its first bytes, so an authentic quote reports `not_found`. Match per text with a bound instead. | `src/saved.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 2 | MEDIUM | Rendered HTML is parsed as a JavaScript string and saved as UTF-8; an unpaired surrogate becomes U+FFFD on disk, so a quote can fail on replay. Round-trip the rendered HTML through UTF-8 before parsing, hashing and saving. | `src/pipeline.ts`, `src/saved.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 3 | MEDIUM | The writer stores URLs of any length, but the verifier's shape check refuses URLs over 8,192 characters, so a read of a very long URL saves a snapshot that never verifies. Share one URL limit: refuse such a save before writing, or accept the full URL in verify. | `src/saved.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 2 | LOW | `clear` counts every planned snapshot as removed even when its deletion failed. Return real outcomes from `applyCleanup`. | `src/saved.ts`, `src/cleanup.ts` |  | f676107 |
| save 4 | LOW | A file warning from a `no-store` response reports `saved.reason: "no_page"` instead of `no_store` (nothing is written either way). Attach `sv.reason` before returning the file warning. | `src/pipeline.ts` |  | f676107 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | A saved rendered read whose rendered HTML hashes the same as the cached copy keeps the cached parse but saves the current render's inspection facts (mark, complete, wall_decided); when the two inspections differ (one complete, one fallen back to HTML), receipts quote the old parse and `saved verify` replays with the new facts, so an honest quote reports `not_found`. Fix: use the fresh parse for a saving read, or include the inspection facts in the cache-equivalence check. | `src/pipeline.ts` (`run`), `src/saved.ts` (`readerTexts`) |  | f676107 |
| integration 1 | LOW | A read with `save` and a section that does not exist returns `status: "error"` but still writes a snapshot (`finishPage` calls `saveNow([])` on the content-error branch), against "errors save nothing". Fix: report `not_saved` (`no_page`) on that branch without writing. | `src/pipeline.ts` (`finishPage`) |  | f676107 |
| integration 3 | MEDIUM | `batchFiles` reads at most 5,000 batch files and `batches` drops its `more` flag, while batches are never removed; past 5,000 batches the older ones vanish from `receipts prove`, `anchor upgrade` and the not-yet-anchored set (a proven receipt cannot be found, an anchored one is sent again). Fix: make every kept batch searchable (stream, or an index), page upgrade scans, and say when a scan was incomplete. | `src/anchor.ts` (`batchFiles`, `batches`) | At one batch a day, about 13 years away | 65b8e55 |
