### v0.7.0 (2026-10-31)

JavaScript-only pages (W-10), translate (W-26), and walls and hidden text revisited with rendering (N-02, N-05, the 2026-10-23 decision). Optional rendering in the installed Google Chrome, driven over a pipe with no new package, owner-only and off by default, every request the page makes fetched under Tiresias's own rules; on a rendered page the browser decides walls and hidden text; every read reports its language, the shield matches phrases aimed at AI in 13 languages, and an owner-set translation hook answers `read` with `translate`. Every request is now capped by the bytes its connection receives, plain reads included. Closes W-10 and W-26.

- **Built as three tracks in series on branch `v0.7`** from 6bff90c (v0.6.1, 802 tests): translate (track 3, baba3bd, 811 tests; Codex round 1: no HIGH), render (track 1, 84ef126, 829 tests; Codex rounds 1 to 6, the byte cap HIGH four times, decided by Banks and fixed at the connection; round 6 clean) and walls (track 2, 0f2b4bd, 835 tests; Codex round 1: no HIGH). Then reviewed together (`docs/reviews/2026-10-31-v07-integration-round-N.md`).
- **Added:** `src/render.ts`, `src/cdp.ts`, `src/inspect.ts`, `src/lang.ts`, `src/shieldphrases.ts`, `src/translate.ts`, `src/hook.ts`; `read` options `render` and `translate` (MCP and CLI); top-level `render`, `language` and `translation`; `untrusted_content.declared_language` and `untrusted_content.translation`; `config.json` keys `rendering` and `translator`; `tiresias translator set|show|off`; `TIRESIAS_RENDERING`, `TIRESIAS_CHROME`; cache entry `rendered`; history `rendered` and `lang`.
- **Changed:** `PARSER_VERSION` 11 to 12 (cached pages are read again once). `safeFetch` counts and caps every request by the bytes its connection receives (`Wire`); Web Risk lookups go through `boundedGet`; a robots.txt budget refusal is never cached. The shield's English phrase list moved to `shieldphrases.ts` with twelve more languages.
- **Decisions:** section 9, rows dated 2026-10-31; full track records in `docs/v0.7/render.md`, `translate.md` and `walls.md`.

#### Deferred to compute-day sweep

Deferred by Travis's rule (fix HIGH only mid-week; MEDIUM and LOW go to the Thursday compute-day sweep). The two walls rows marked first give false hidden-text flags on honest pages (a dark-scheme page, a `display: contents` wrapper), so they go first.

| Track, round | Severity | Summary | File | Note | Closed (v0.9.1) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | `display: contents` wrappers fail `checkVisibility()` although their content shows: direct text is marked `not_displayed`, and the ancestor climb can move a hidden child's mark onto a visible wrapper, hiding its siblings. Fix: judge text by its ranges; consolidate only at ancestors that actually hide their contents. | `src/inspect.ts` (`judge`, hidden-root grouping) | **First on Thursday** (false hidden-text flags on honest pages) | 2bf4cb9 |
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | `bgAt` assumes a white canvas; an honest dark-scheme page (`color-scheme: dark`, transparent backgrounds, browser light text) can have all its text flagged `same_color` and removed. Fix: resolve the canvas colour for the used colour scheme. | `src/inspect.ts` (`bgAt`) | **First on Thursday** (false hidden-text flags on honest pages) | 2bf4cb9 |
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | Form controls named `checkVisibility` or `nodeType` shadow those properties on their form (named-property access works in the isolated world too); `vis` catches the failure and says visible, `effOpacity` skips the form, so text directly inside a transparent form can pass as visible. Fix: call native methods and getters captured from the isolated world's prototypes. | `src/inspect.ts` (`inspectScript`: `vis`, `effOpacity`) |  | 2bf4cb9, 136d789 |
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | Marker loops stop at 2,000 inputs or CAPTCHA candidates without saying so, so a visible password box after 2,000 hidden inputs gives no marker and a definitive `walls: "rendered"` no-wall verdict. Fix: track marker-scan completion; after truncation, no definitive negative verdict (HTML fallback unless a marker already proves a wall). | `src/inspect.ts` (marker loops, `wallVerdict`) |  | 2bf4cb9 |
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | Hidden text inside raw-text elements (`xmp`, `plaintext`) is wrapped in `thidden`, which htmlparser2 reparses as literal text, so the mark is lost and (inspection complete) the words are shown unflagged. Fix: mark the raw-text element itself, or carry text-node decisions in a form that survives serialization. | `src/inspect.ts` (mark insertion) |  | 2bf4cb9 |
| walls 1 | MEDIUM | Only `rgb()`/`rgba()` computed colours are parsed; transparent text or an opaque cover written in `color()`/`oklch()` skips the `same_color` and `covered` checks. Fix: normalize computed colours with alpha; an unparsed colour makes the inspection incomplete. | `src/inspect.ts` (`rgba`, `judge`, `opaque`) |  | 2bf4cb9 |
| walls 1 | LOW | Any `background-image` counts as opaque, including transparent gradients, so a decorative layer above text gives false `covered` flags. Fix: require evidence of opacity. | `src/inspect.ts` (`opaque`) |  | designed away (section 9, 2026-11-01; Travis); two fix attempts (02c9b44, 1eab0ad) reverted in d3c4016 |
| walls 1 | LOW | The rendered fingerprint hashes HTML containing the random mark name, so re-rendering an unchanged page with hidden text looks changed (provenance reset, empty diff). Fix: canonicalize the mark name before hashing. | `src/pipeline.ts` (`run`) |  | 02c9b44 |
| walls 1 | LOW | A cached rendered reply rebuilds only `{status: "cached"}` and loses `walls`, `hidden_text` and `overlay` (the consent-box sentence too). Fix: keep the fixed inspection codes with the cache entry and restore them. | `src/pipeline.ts` (`run`, `finishPage`) |  | 02c9b44 |
| walls 1 | LOW | A large transparent fixed layer gets no `overlay` note: `"other"` needs opaque coverage of most of the text. Fix: report a detected overlay on its own, with its own fixed sentence (and align the plan's known-limits wording). | `src/inspect.ts` (`wallVerdict`) |  | 02c9b44, 75e6409 |
| render 1 | MEDIUM | Cache shortcuts can skip a required re-render: an explicit `render` on an already rendered copy can be served fresh or by a 304, and `check` (even `--fresh`) can reuse the rendered parse via `plain_hash`, missing changes only in scripts or data | `src/pipeline.ts` (run: `rerender`, `reuse`) |  | faf5038; the fresh-copy part designed away (section 9, 2026-10-31) |
| render 1 | MEDIUM | A failed render replaces the last good rendered copy with the plain one, and a check can then report its content as removed | `src/pipeline.ts` (run, render step) |  | faf5038 |
| render 1 | MEDIUM | The profile sweep removes a folder over an hour old even when its owning process is alive (a suspended render) | `src/render.ts` (`sweepProfiles`) |  | faf5038 |
| render 1 | MEDIUM | A NUL-terminated CDP message is assembled and parsed without checking it against `MAX_MESSAGE` (only unterminated tails are checked) | `src/cdp.ts` (`take`) |  | faf5038 |
| render 1 | LOW | Setup before the `try` (timer, temp folder, proxy) is not cleaned up if a later setup step fails | `src/render.ts` (`withRenderedPage`) |  | faf5038 |
| render 2 | MEDIUM | A redirected subresource is handed to the page under its original URL (Tiresias follows the redirect itself), so a redirected module resolves relative imports against the wrong base and a cross-origin redirect is invisible to Chrome's CORS checks | `src/render.ts` (`fetchFor`) |  | faf5038 |
| render 1 | LOW | Timers of the losing side of two races (the 3 s evaluate timer, the 2 s exit wait) are not cleared | `src/render.ts` |  | faf5038 |
| translate 1 | LOW | A read that ends in a file warning with Office text ignores `translate` (no translation, no `translation` status). Run the hook after the Office reply's final size and receipt filtering, on reads only, with `shownText` also collecting `untrusted_content.inside` text. | `src/filereply.ts`, `src/pipeline.ts`, `src/translate.ts` |  | 3f99ef1 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | Hidden `textarea` text on a rendered page: the inspection skips textareas but the reader keeps their text, and with the inspection complete the HTML hiding checks are off, so a hidden textarea's text is read unflagged (and reaches language detection, the translation input, receipts and the cached parse). Fix: align the inspection's and the reader's exclusions, or judge the textarea element and mark it. | `src/inspect.ts` (`inspectScript`), `src/reader.ts`, `src/shield.ts` (`hiddenReason`) | | 02c9b44 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | `resume` drops `render` and `translate`: handoff tickets keep only the question and section, so a rendered read stopped at a wall resumes unrendered (an empty shell can be accepted and the ticket deleted), and a translation asked for is lost on either resume path. Fix: keep and re-check both options in the ticket and restore them. | `src/engine.ts` (`read`), `src/resume.ts` (`resume`) | | 3f99ef1 |
| integration 1 | MEDIUM | A cancel or the call deadline during the translation hook fails the read after the cache entry and receipts were already written. Fix: build receipts first, but persist and record them only after the translation and the final cancellation check. | `src/pipeline.ts` (`finishPage`) | | 3f99ef1 |
| integration 2 | MEDIUM | On a rendered page, text whose own element has `pointer-events: none` is never judged `clipped` (by design, to avoid false flags), so fully clipped text with such an element can stay unmarked: read as shown (cache, receipts, language, translation input) and counted as visible text against a login-wall verdict. Fix: measure clipping apart from hit-test eligibility. | `src/inspect.ts` (`pointState`) | | 017f0d6, fe1a96b (section 9, 2026-11-01; Travis) |
| integration 3 | LOW | A refetch whose body is byte-identical keeps the cached parse, so a changed `Content-Language` (even with `fresh`) is not picked up: the old declaration is returned and saved, and where it decides the language the translation hook gets the wrong source or skips as `same_language`. Fix: refresh the language from the current headers when reusing a parse (304 too). | `src/pipeline.ts` (`run`) | | faf5038 |

### v0.6.0 (tagged 2026-10-26; sweep released as v0.6.1 on 2026-10-30)

Inside files, plus browser basics. Zip listing without unpacking, Office text without macros (with receipts, section paging and question search), and calendar events from `.ics` files; tabs and back/forward (W-24), the downloads shelf (W-27), site settings per host (W-25), the `tiresias settings` command (W-29) and a fast first read with the door checks in the background (W-30). Closes W-24, W-25, W-27, W-29, W-30 and the "Not in v0.5" items of section 4.4.

- **Built as three parallel tracks** (`v0.6-browse` 70bddc7, `v0.6-settings` 201b728, `v0.6-files` afe1e89, all from 9e34f0d), each reviewed by Codex on its own branch (browse: clean at round 11; settings: clean at round 12; files: no HIGH in 15 rounds, round 15 deferred), then merged into `v0.6` and reviewed together (`docs/reviews/2026-10-26-v06-integration-round-N.md`).
- **Added:** `tabs`, `tab` and `files` MCP tools; `tiresias tabs | tab | back | forward | files | settings`; `file_warning.inside` and `untrusted_content.inside`; `untrusted_content.events`; top-level `tab`, `shelf`, `doors_pending` and `site_settings`; `config.json` keys `doors` and `sites`; `TIRESIAS_MAX_INSPECT_BYTES`.
- **Changed:** `PARSER_VERSION` 10 to 11 (cached pages are read again once). A first read no longer waits for the door checks unless `doors` is `wait`.
- **Decisions:** section 9, rows dated 2026-10-26; full track records in `docs/v0.6/`.

Round 2 was told of that decision and found no HIGH. Travis confirmed the exemption on 2026-11-01 (section 9).

  - [x] round 90 (HIGH 4, not clean; the last round allowed): combined meaning split across units (e.g. field `security` labelled "code"; "Card" and "number" in two labels; ancestor titles), which the deleted field-walk label check used to catch; reference lists split on non-ASCII spaces (U+00A0 in an id); script and style elements missing from the id index; zero-width characters inside "Password". Travis chose (a): all four fixed, commit e58ca14.
  - [x] round 91 (HIGH 2, MEDIUM 1, LOW 1, not clean): the per-control word bag still misses meaning reached through referenced elements' attributes, transitive and form-level references, intervening ancestors, the form's own text and inline-joined text; the scattered vocabulary misses some phrase forms (signin + code, sign + incode, no + card); duplicated context sets make the bag check quadratic (contained by the worker deadline); a long `<pre>` of links is cut without notice (reader). Travis chose (a): all four fixed, commit 00dd03e.
  - [x] round 92 (HIGH 2, not clean): the visit-once rule skips nodes a reference reached first, losing a joined word (a span inside an inline "Password" label) or the nested_control check (an ancestor referencing a control inside a template); the shared marker list dropped round 90's "not" + "robot" rule ("I am not robot"). Both fixed (coordinator: option a), commit 652c6ca.
  - [x] round 93 (HIGH 2, LOW 1, not clean): mixed capitals ("PassWord", "CvV", "CaPtChA") are split by the camelCase rule before lowercasing, so the marker word never forms; joining an element's attribute values can run a card number into other digits (20 digits), which the card check ignores; a `<pre>` reads boilerplate text (class "advert") that the normal walk suppresses, so the round-92 claim behind deleting the backstop was wrong (oversized pages are still refused later by scanHead). All three fixed (coordinator: option a), commit 18f6bef.
  - [x] round 94 (2026-10-24; HIGH 2, not clean): an invisible character between "my" and "Password" defeats the camelCase split (invisible characters are removed after it); mixed capitals inside a compound name ("myPaSsWoRd", "myCvV", "mySeCuRiTyCode") leave no token equal to the marker. Both fixed (coordinator: option a), commit 098320a.
  - [x] round 95 (HIGH 2, not clean): many-word markers in odd capitals or with letters split by dots ("SeCuRiTy Code", "ArE YoU HuMaN", "S.e.c.u.r.i.t.y Code") are missed, a regression from deleting the unsplit word variant in round 94; a card number split across adjacent inline text nodes (each under 13 digits) is missed, since digits are checked per node only. Both fixed (coordinator: option a, skeleton design), commit ab83e4c.
  - [x] round 96 (HIGH 1, LOW 1, not clean): a literal line break in the page's own text splits a block, because block boundaries are marked with "\n" and the joined text is split on "\n" ("pass" + LF + "word", "rou" + LF + "ting", and a card number split by one LF across two spans pass); a wrapping label's attributes are judged twice (ancestor check and root walk), so a long harmless title reaches too_large. Both fixed (coordinator: option a), commit 150ab56.
  - [x] round 97 (LOW 2, not clean; NO form-guard finding): search bigrams are built from UTF-16 units, so a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane makes a bogus half-pair token (reader); a text-mode (`--text`) CLI error goes to standard output instead of standard error (cli). Both fixed (coordinator: option a, carry nothing forward), commit 0175667.
  - [x] round 98 (2026-10-24): CLEAN (HIGH 0, MEDIUM 0, LOW 0). Released: version 0.5.1, merged to main, tagged v0.5.1.

Scope: the findings of the 2026-10-20 structure and spec review (`reviews/2026-10-20-structure-review.md`; read-only, by Banks with four Opus 5.5 reviewers, one per slice of the source). Nothing else goes in. Order of work is the order below; each item gets a test that fails without it, as before. Codex review follows the round budget (a small cap set up front, ship when no HIGH).

**1. Permission slips bind a person (W-05 hardened).**
- `slip grant` and `slip revoke` refuse unless standard input is a terminal and the person types the slip's site back to confirm. An agent running the CLI from a shell cannot pass this on its own. On macOS, when available, the slip key is held in the Keychain and signing asks for Touch ID (planned as an option, not required for v0.5.1).
- Uses are tamper-evident: each `.use-N` file holds the slip id, the use number and a signature; a use file that does not verify counts as used.
- Revocation survives a copied slip: revoked ids go to `slips/revoked.jsonl` (append only, kept until the slip would have expired), and `checkSlip` refuses an id on that list.
- The spec says in W-05 that an agent with a shell is trusted like the owner for everything except granting.

**2. Five cheap safety fixes (N-06, N-10).**
- Policy waits (Crawl-delay, cooldown) run on the call's 60-second deadline, not the 15-second request timer (`safety.ts` `timeoutMs`). Today a site with `Crawl-delay: 20` is reported "The site took too long to answer."
- An https to http redirect is refused (`downgrade` error); signed headers never go out on plain http.
- Ports: only 80, 443 and 8000 to 8999 on public addresses by default (`TIRESIAS_ALLOW_PORTS` to widen); the rest refused as `port_blocked`.
- `TIRESIAS_ALLOW_PRIVATE` is honored only when `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` is set or through a test-only entry point; production builds ignore it.
- Redirects followed only on 301, 302, 303, 307 and 308; `Content-Encoding: deflate` falls back to raw deflate.

**3. Default politeness (N-06).**
- One request at a time per host and at least 1 second between requests to the same host by default, shared across processes through a `hosts/` spacing file in the store (the Retry-After log already works this way); `Crawl-delay` raises it. Discovery probes (`doors.ts`) run through the same queue, so a first read is no longer a burst of about eight requests.
- The cooldown log is cached in memory and re-read only when its size or mtime changes (as `safebrowsing.ts` does); `lastHit`, `chains`, `waiting` and the robots cache are bounded (LRU, 1,000 hosts).

**4. Structure.**
- `engine.ts` split into `envelope.ts` (reply building, budget, error mapping), `pagecache.ts`, `pipeline.ts` (run, finishPage, content), `filereply.ts` (file warnings and the scanner call), `resume.ts`, `watchflow.ts`, `formflow.ts`; `engine.ts` keeps only the entry points. No behavior change; the existing tests are the proof.
- Errors get a common base (`TiresiasError` with a `kind`), so `guarded` and `doorsOnly` map by kind instead of hand-synced `instanceof` lists. Bad settings (`positiveInt`, `budgetMs`, `workerHeapMb`) raise a `ConfigError` with the setting's name, never "internal error".
- `formSubmit` calls the same pre-send check as `politeFetch` instead of its own copy; `hashPieces` uses `readBody`'s sink. `sandbox.ts` no longer imports from `reader.ts`. The two ambient contexts (`safety.ts` AsyncLocalStorage, `store.ts` private flag) become one `CallContext`.
- Private mode fails closed in the store: `appendLine` and `writeJson` refuse when the call is private, the cooldown line excepted. One way of saying "private" (`ctx.private`).
- One helper for "one JSON file per item in a checked folder" (temp file, fsync, rename; size-capped reads), used by bookmarks, handoffs and slips. Tickets and bookmarks are no longer written in place. `clear --all` leaves `.tmp` files younger than a day alone. `resume` claims its ticket first.
- Every on-disk record carries `v` and, in the receipts file, `kind` (`quote`, `form`, `file`). `verify` accepts a list of known slip versions. Watch baselines are kept outside the page cache's retention (a bookmark's last fingerprint lives in the bookmark file).
- Daily log export escapes Markdown in purpose, agent and URL text (no image, link or wikilink can render from a page's words).
- The PDF child starts with Node's `--permission` flags: file reads only in the pdfjs-dist and app folders, no child processes, no workers.

**5. Reader and shield (W-22, W-23, N-02).**
- W-22 as registered: main-content scoring, boilerplate roles and classes, tables as rows, `pre` kept, list markers, h5 and h6, metadata, Unicode search tokens.
- W-23 as registered: `Accept` prefers markdown; `llms-full.txt` fetched and used when listed.
- Shield: a cap per flag type (not one combined cap), with `flags_dropped` counts; the page's own `<style>` rules read for `display:none`, `visibility:hidden`, `font-size:0`, `opacity:0`; `sr-only` and `visually-hidden` class names; zero-width, bidirectional and Unicode tag characters flagged as `invisible_characters`; phrases matched on text normalized (NFKC, zero-width stripped); hidden PDF text (white, tiny or off-page items, from pdf.js positions) flagged as `hidden_text`.
- Ladder: a sitemap alone no longer makes `best` "feed"; `door` means "used" everywhere and `doors` reports `available` separately; the needs report counts a better door only when one exists that Tiresias could consume.
- Diffs: JSON and JSON-LD are diffed by line (pretty-printed, one value per line), not fixed 4,000-character slices.
- Walls: the login and CAPTCHA text checks look at the first 4,000 visible characters and at every form, not at a whole-page length threshold.

**6. Repo hygiene, done now so that v1.0 (the public launch) is a release and not a scramble.** The repo stays private until v1.0; nothing here changes that.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (build and test on macOS and Linux, Node 22; timing tests marked and skipped on CI), `SECURITY.md` (how to report, what counts as HIGH, the 90-day disclosure window), `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (generated from this section), `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `examples/`.
- MCP: every tool gets `title` and annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint` on `form_submit`, `openWorldHint` on anything that fetches); the shared policy text moves once into the server's `instructions`; tool descriptions are trimmed to how to call the tool; `receipts`, `history` and `log` get the same envelope and page fields as every other reply; error replies carry the URL the caller passed.
- CLI: exit 0 only on `ok` and `unchanged`, 2 on `wall`, `flagged`, `refused`, `file_warning`, 1 on error; `--version`; per-command `--help`; options checked per command (an option a command does not take is an error); errors on stderr as one JSON line when `--text` is not set.
- Packaging: `private` dropped, `files` lists `dist` (without `dist/test`), `repository`, `homepage`, `bugs`, `keywords`; the `@napi-rs/canvas` stub becomes a published placeholder package or the README's install uses `--omit=optional` with the lazy-load guard kept. `.gitignore` gains `.DS_Store`, `*.tgz`, `coverage/`. `reviews/` and this file move under `docs/`.
- README rewritten for a first-time visitor: one-paragraph pitch, 30-second install, sample output, feature list by what it does (not by version), an honest comparison with Playwright MCP, fetch MCP, Browser Use and Jina Reader, the user agent string read from the package version.
- Tests renamed by feature (`cache`, `robots`, `cooldown`, `worker`, `handoff`, `profiles`, `files`, `slips`, `forms`, `mcp`, `cli`), MCP tests cover every tool over the protocol, the CLI tests check exit codes, and one test covers HTTPS address pinning.
- Register drift fixed (done 2026-10-20 in this spec: N-07, W-13, W-14, W-15, W-17 now `shipped (v0.2.0)`); section 4.4's "never by the name or Content-Type" reworded to match the 2026-10-20 builder's decision (names and types decide text kinds) (done 2026-10-21 in this spec).

**Carried from v0.5.0 (round 68):** the `#!` before `koly` trailer precedence (MEDIUM) and the quarantine-sweep accounting (LOW) are fixed in v0.5.1, not v0.6.

**Work log (as built):**
- **A. Round-68 carry-overs, fixed:** the disk-image trailer is checked before the `#!` line in `signature()` (after the program marks), so a `#!` body with a `koly` trailer is a file warning (kind `archive`); `clear` and the daily trim list stale quarantine copies BEFORE planning the disk cap (`staleQuarantine`), so the room they free counts and their bytes are in `total_bytes_freed` and `store_bytes_after`. Tests in `v051.test.ts`, each failing without its fix (3 mutations checked: the trailer moved back after `#!`, the swept bytes left out of the cap plan, the swept bytes left out of the total). Tests 415 to 417.
- **B. Item 2, five safety fixes:** (1) the request timer (15 s) is paused while the policy work of a hop runs (robots.txt, the Crawl-delay queue), which runs on the call's 60-second deadline instead; (2) a redirect from https to plain http is refused (`downgrade:`), and signing headers are added only to https requests (`mayCarrySignature`); (3) `assertPort` refuses any port but 80, 443 and 8000 to 8999 (`port_blocked:`), `TIRESIAS_ALLOW_PORTS` adds ports or ranges; (4) `TIRESIAS_ALLOW_PRIVATE` counts only when `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` is set (`testMode()`); (5) only 301, 302, 303, 307 and 308 are followed (`redirectTarget`), and `Content-Encoding: deflate` is read as zlib or raw deflate by its first two bytes. Two older tests used port 81 for a host that never answers; they now use allowed ports. Tests in `v051.test.ts`, each failing without its fix (7 mutations checked: the timer pause, the downgrade check, the http signing rule, the port check, the test-runner condition, the redirect status list, raw deflate). Tests 417 to 423.
- **C. Item 1, slips bind a person (Touch ID and Keychain skipped, optional per scope):** `person.ts` `confirmByTyping`: `slip grant` and `slip revoke` refuse (fixed text, nothing written) unless standard input is a terminal and the person types the slip's site back (the origin as Tiresias writes it; for `revoke <id>` the slip file's site, or the id when the file is damaged; for `--all`, `revoke all`); the prompt goes to standard error. Private mode is still checked first. Each `.use-N` holds `v`, the slip id, the use number, the time, the label and an Ed25519 signature by the slip key (`useVerifies`); uses taken are counted up to the HIGHEST use number present and claims go upward from it, so deleting a lower use file gives nothing back. `slips/revoked.jsonl` (append only, fsynced, written BEFORE the slip file is renamed away; also for slips retired by cleanup) holds every revoked id until the slip would have expired (30 days for a damaged one); a slip on it is damaged: counted, never used; cleanup prunes past lines (temp file and rename). A list that cannot be read fails loudly (no slip is used). Tests: a pseudo-terminal runner (`runAsPerson` in `helper.ts`, Python's `pty`); the existing CLI slip tests now grant and revoke as a person. 3 new tests, each failing without its fix (7 mutations checked: the terminal check, the typed match, highest-use counting, claims going upward, the revoked-list check, writing the revoked line, pruning). Tests 423 to 426.
- **D. Item 3, default politeness:** every request to a site (pages, discovery probes, robots.txt) waits its turn in one per-site queue (`queue.ts`, moved out of `fetcher.ts`) and starts at least 1 second after the one before (`hosts.ts` `spacingMs`), or the site's Crawl-delay when larger. The slot is reserved in a shared spacing file, `hosts/<hash>` at the store root (a short O_EXCL lock, broken after 5 s; temp file and rename), so processes side by side space their requests as one queue; private calls and `form_preview` read it but never write it; `clear` and the daily trim delete spacing files over a day old; `hosts/` is shared by every profile and not counted by the disk cap. Probes that ask for robots.txt at once share one fetch (non-private). The cooldown log is read again only when one of its files changes (size, mtime, inode). `lastHit`, the queue, the waiting sets and the robots cache are `LruMap`s of at most 1,000 sites, and a queue nobody waits in is forgotten. Test-only: `TIRESIAS_HOST_SPACING_MS` (honored only under Node's test runner; the helper sets 0). Tests: 5 new, each failing without its fix (8 mutations checked: the default spacing, robots.txt in the queue, the shared robots fetch, the spacing file, the cooldown cache, the bounded map, forgetting an empty queue; and the earlier slip and preview tests caught the spacing file being written by a read-only call). Tests 426 to 431.
- **E7. Daily log escapes Markdown:** purpose, agent name and URL text go into the exported Markdown as code spans fenced with one more backtick than their longest backtick run (`dailylog.ts` `code`), so no image, link, wikilink, HTML or bare-URL autolink renders from them. Test fails without it (1 mutation checked); two W-13 tests updated to the code-span format. Tests 431 to 432.

- **E8. PDF child under Node's permission model:** the child is started with `--permission`, `--allow-fs-read` for Tiresias's `dist/` and for the `node_modules` folder that holds pdfjs-dist (real paths too), and `--allow-worker` (`pdfproc.ts` `childExecArgv`). It cannot read the store (keys), write anything, start a process or load a native add-on. Test runs the same flags and checks a key file, a write and a process are denied and the app is readable (1 mutation checked: the flags dropped; the fork using `childExecArgv` is not isolable beyond that). Tests 432 to 433.
- **E4. Private mode fails closed in the store:** `writeJson` and `appendLine` throw `PrivateWriteError` inside a private call (the cooldown line excepted), so a call site that forgets its own check cannot write; the spacing file, slips and the one-file-per-item helper have their own private checks. Test fails without each check (2 mutations checked). Tests 433 to 434.
- **E2. One error base:** `TiresiasError` (abstract, `kind`) in `errors.ts`; every error Tiresias raises on purpose extends it (safety, fetch, refused, wall, payment, signing, pdf, flagged, safe_browsing, slip, config, profile, complex, page_too_complex, provided, handoff, bookmark, private_write). `guarded` maps an error to its reply in one `switch` on the kind (`errorEnv`); `doorsOnly` rethrows every Tiresias error but a plain fetch failure. `ConfigError` moved to `errors.ts` (re-exported by `config.ts`); `positiveInt` (TIRESIAS_MAX_BYTES, TIRESIAS_MAX_FILE_BYTES), `budgetMs`, `workerHeapMb` and `pdfMemoryMb` raise it with the setting's name, so a bad setting is an `error` reply that names it, never "internal error". Change in `doors`: a profile, slip, PDF or setting error during the page fetch now fails the call instead of being read as "no page". Test fails without each part (3 mutations checked). Tests 434 to 435.
- **E5. One helper for item files:** `itemfiles.ts` (`readItem`: no links, no blocking, ours, size cap before parsing; `writeItem`: exclusive 0600 temp file, written whole, fsynced, then linked to its name (create, EEXIST when taken) or renamed over it (replace); refuses in private mode; `removeItem`). Bookmarks (add, watch on or off, and the new watch fingerprint), handoff tickets and slips all use it: nothing is written in place. Ticket and bookmark files gain `v: 1`. `clear --all` keeps `.tmp` files younger than a day (another process's write in progress). `resume` claims its ticket first: the file is renamed to `.claimed-<id>.<random>` in one step, deleted when the resume solved it and linked back otherwise; a claim older than 2 minutes (a process that died) is put back when tickets are next read. Tests: 3 new, each failing without its fix (5 mutations checked: the claim rename, putting an unsolved ticket back, young `.tmp` under `--all`, replace written in place, the private check); one W-06 test now expects `v`. Tests 435 to 438.
- **E6. Record versions:** history lines, receipts records, cooldown lines, spacing files, tickets and bookmarks carry `v: 1` (slips already had it); receipts records carry `kind`: `quote`, `form` or `file`. The file-warning record's own file kind moved from `kind` to `file_kind` (older lines: `file: "warning"` still marks them, and their `kind` is the file kind). Slips verify against `SLIP_VERSIONS` (`[1]`). Watch baselines: `watch` keeps the page's fingerprint in the bookmark file (`last_fingerprint`); when the cached copy is gone (the cache keeps pages 30 days), a matching fingerprint counts as unchanged and a different one as changed, with fixed text saying what changed is not known (`BASELINE_GONE`). Tests: 2 new (4 mutations checked: `v` on history, `v` and `kind` on receipts, keeping the fingerprint, the unchanged case); three older tests now expect `v` and `file_kind`. Tests 438 to 440.
- **E3. One of each:** `policyCheck` in `fetcher.ts` is the one pre-send check (a Retry-After wait, robots.txt and a wall in front of it, a Crawl-delay over 30 s), used by `politeFetch` before every hop and by `form_submit` before it claims a slip (its own copy was removed; a cooldown is now checked first). `FileIntake` in `safety.ts` is the one way a file is taken in piece by piece (cap, hash, quarantine copy), used by `readBody` and by `hashPieces` for a saved file. `ComplexityError` and `MAX_NODES` moved to `errors.ts`, so `sandbox.ts` no longer imports `reader.ts`. `context.ts` holds ONE `CallContext` (AsyncLocalStorage) for the call scope and the private flag; `isPrivate()` also counts a private session (`TIRESIAS_PRIVATE`), the one way of asking. Tests: 2 new (3 mutations checked: the context merge, the shared Crawl-delay check, the intake's cap, the last caught by the v0.5 file tests). Tests 440 to 442.
- **E1. Engine split (behavior-neutral):** `engine.ts` (1,673 lines) became `envelope.ts` (reply types, envelope and budget, the error map, the wall ticket, `guarded`), `pagecache.ts` (the cache record), `pipeline.ts` (source choice, fetch, parse, `run`, `finishPage`, content, `doorsOnly`), `filereply.ts` (file warnings, the scanner call, a saved file's intake), `resume.ts`, `watchflow.ts` (bookmarks and watch) and `formflow.ts`; `engine.ts` (83 lines) keeps the entry points and re-exports the rest, so the CLI, the MCP server and the tests call it as before. The modules import one way (pagecache, envelope, filereply, pipeline, then the flows): no cycles. The proof is the unchanged suite: 442 of 442 before and after, no test edited.
- **F1. W-23:** every request sends `Accept: text/markdown, text/html;q=0.9, */*;q=0.8` (`safety.ts` `ACCEPT`); a page answered as `text/markdown` is read as markdown and its `door_detail` is `markdown`. When llms.txt links to `/llms-full.txt` on its own site and the site serves it, a read of the site root uses it (`door_detail` `llms-full.txt`, new value); `DOORS_VERSION` 3 (the discovery cache is re-probed). Tests: 2 new (4 mutations checked: the header, the markdown detail, using the full file, the "listed" rule). Tests 442 to 444.
- **F2. Shield (N-02):** the cap of 30 flags is per flag type, and the flags past it are counted per type in a new top-level `flags_dropped` (and on the parsed page); 30 HTML comments no longer push out an injection flag. The page's own `<style>` elements are read (never run) for `.class` and `#id` rules that set `display:none`, `visibility:hidden`, `font-size:0` or `opacity:0` (comments stripped, `@` blocks skipped, 256 KB and 5,000 rules at most), and such elements are hidden text like an inline style; the conventional screen-reader class names (`sr-only`, `visually-hidden` and similar) are hidden text too. Zero-width, bidirectional-control and Unicode tag characters in any scanned text raise a new flag, `invisible_characters`; phrases are matched on the text NFKC-normalized with those characters stripped (first 200,000 characters). PDFs: text drawn in white (from the page's drawing operations, following the fill colour through save and restore), text under 1 point (from pdf.js positions) and text drawn off the page (which pdf.js leaves out of its text) are flagged `hidden_text` with the page number; white and tiny text stay in the page text. Tests: 2 new (8 mutations checked: the per-type cap, the style rules, sr-only, the invisible-character flag, NFKC, white, tiny and off-page PDF text). Tests 444 to 446.
- **F3. Walls:** the whole-page length thresholds (1,200 visible characters for a CAPTCHA, 800 for a password box) are gone. A challenge widget (`cf-turnstile`), a CAPTCHA widget or a CAPTCHA inside a form, or a password box, is a wall when it is what the top of the page is about: fewer than 1,200 visible characters (800 for a password) come before it, and no `<main>` or `<article>` with at least 1,200 visible characters (its footer and nav not counted) follows it. Every password box (up to 50) is looked at, not only the first. A non-200 answer with a challenge or CAPTCHA anywhere is a wall as before. All scans stay `indexOf` over the bounded prefix. Test: a login page padded with 10 KB of links below the form is a wall; an article with a captcha'd comment form and a forum page with a sign-in box in its header are read (3 mutations checked: the old whole-page rule, the content-after exception, the text-before limit). Tests 446 to 447.
- **F4. Ladder:** a sitemap in robots.txt alone no longer makes the best door `feed` (`summarize`); a `doors` reply has `door: "none"` and a new top-level `available` (the best door offered), so `door` means the door used everywhere; the needs report counts a better door only when it is one Tiresias reads (`text` or `structured`), not MCP, API or feed doors, which are found but not read. The W-12 test now seeds a structured-data site and expects only it. Test: 1 new (3 mutations checked: the sitemap rule, `door` on doors, the consumable filter). Tests 447 to 448.
- **F5. JSON diffs by line:** a JSON body and the JSON-LD section of a page are pretty-printed and kept as blocks of whole lines (`lineBlocks`): one line per block up to 5,000 lines; past that, runs of lines that end where a cheap hash of the line says (content-defined, so a change only moves the blocks around it); a line over 4,000 characters is cut. `PARSER_VERSION` is 10 (older cached copies are read again). Test: inserting one item near the top of a 300-item JSON list is a handful of changed blocks, not the whole body (1 mutation checked). Tests 448 to 449.
- **F6. W-22 reader mode, written in-house (no library):** main-content scoring (`chooseMain`: every element's text and link text summed in one pass from the leaves; a single `<main>` or `role=main` with real text, else the `<article>` with the most text, else the `div`, `section` or `td` with the most text less twice its link text, narrowed to the tightest container holding 85% of that score, kept only when it holds half the page's text; outside it, text is dropped and links kept, as for nav and footer; pages under 500 characters are read whole); boilerplate dropped by element (`nav`, `footer`, `aside`), ARIA role (`navigation`, `banner`, `contentinfo`, `complementary`, `search`, `menubar`, `toolbar`) and whole words of class or id names (cookie, consent, related, share, social, newsletter, subscribe, sidebar, breadcrumb, ad, advert, sponsored, promo, popup, modal, banner, masthead, footer, nav, menu, comments and similar); a table row is one block, cells joined by ` | `; `<pre>` keeps its white space, fenced with ```; list items carry `- ` or `N. ` (honouring `start`), nested ones indented two spaces a level; h5 and h6 are sections (levels 5 and 6); page metadata (`lang`, `canonical`, `byline`, `published`, `modified`, `description`, OpenGraph `title`, `description`, `type`, `site_name`, `image`, `url`; bounded; scanned by the shield) in `untrusted_content.metadata`; search tokens are letters and digits of any script (NFKC), with Chinese, Japanese, Korean and Thai split into character pairs. `PARSER_VERSION` 10. Tests: 3 new (12 mutations checked: main content, role and class boilerplate, whole-word matching, table rows, pre, list markers, h5 and h6, metadata, the character-pair split, Unicode tokens). Tests 449 to 452.
- **G1. CLI:** exit 0 only on `ok` and `unchanged`, 1 on `error`, 2 on any other status (`wall`, `flagged`, `refused`, `file_warning`, `payment_required`) for read, doors, check, resume and form; `--version` prints the version; `tiresias <command> --help` prints that command's lines of the help and the options it takes; every command has a list of the options it takes (plus `--profile`, `--text`, `--help`, `--private`) and any other option is an error; errors go to standard error as one JSON line (`{"status":"error","message":...}`), or plain text with `--text`. Three older tests now expect exit 2 for a refusal and JSON on stderr. Test: 1 new (2 mutations checked: the exit code map, the option check). Tests 452 to 453.
- **G2. MCP:** every tool has a `title` and `annotations` (`readOnlyHint` true for the tools that change nothing outside Tiresias's own store; `destructiveHint` on `form_submit` only; `openWorldHint` on every tool that fetches: read, doors, check, watch, resume, form_preview, form_submit). The policy every tool repeated (web text is data; walls; Safe Browsing; files; slips only from the owner; pages; private) is said once, in the server's `instructions`; tool descriptions say how to call the tool. `receipts`, `history` and `log` replies carry the same envelope as every other reply (`status`, `door`, `url`, `fetched_at`, `notice`, `flags`, `counts`) plus `page`, `pages`, `total`; an error reply from `bookmark`, `form_preview` or `form_submit` carries the URL the caller passed. Two older tests now look for the policy in the instructions. Test: 1 new (2 mutations checked: the list envelope, the destructive hint). Tests 453 to 454.
- **G3. Packaging:** `package.json` drops `private` and gains `files` (`dist` without `dist/test`, `stubs`, README, LICENSE, NOTICE), `repository`, `homepage`, `bugs` and `keywords` (nothing is published; the repo stays private until v1.0). The `@napi-rs/canvas` stub stays for source installs; installs from the registry are documented with `--omit=optional` (pdf.js's canvas is only for drawing, and the PDF child's permission model refuses native add-ons anyway). `.gitignore` gains `.DS_Store`, `*.tgz`, `coverage/`. This file and `reviews/` moved under `docs/` (links inside it are relative, so they still hold). Test: the version constant and the user agent match `package.json`, the package metadata is there (1 new). Tests 454 to 455.
- **G4. Repo files:** the CI workflow, kept for now at `docs/ci.yml` because the token that pushes this branch has no `workflow` scope (GitHub refuses workflow files without it; moving it to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` after `gh auth refresh -s workflow` turns CI on), builds and tests on Ubuntu and macOS, Node 22, `npm ci` then `npm test` with `CI=true`); timing upper bounds in the tests go through `timing()` in `src/test/helper.ts`, which skips them when `CI` is set (29 bounds in 11 files; the behaviour around them is still tested). `SECURITY.md` (GitHub private vulnerability reporting, what counts as HIGH, the 90-day window), `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (built from this section by `scripts/changelog.mjs`), `examples/` (an MCP client config, a read-and-quote script, a cron line for watch). No new test.
- **G5. Tests:** files renamed by feature (`engine` to `pipeline`, `round3` to `robots`, `round4` to `cooldown`, `round6` to `walls`, `round7` to `worker`, `round8` to `worker-search`, `v02` to `log-private-limits`, `v03` to `watch-diffs-needs`, `v03b` to `handoff-cache`, `v04` to `profiles`, `v05` to `files`, `v051` to `review-fixes`; `slips`, `forms`, `mcp`, `pdf`, `safety`, `signing`, `safebrowsing` already were). Files were renamed whole, not split test by test, so some still hold a few tests of a neighbouring feature. `mcp.test.ts` calls every one of the 15 tools over the protocol (and checks the list it calls is the list the server offers); the CLI exit codes are tested (G1); `safety.test.ts` has an HTTPS test: a throwaway certificate for `pinned.test` made with openssl, a local TLS server, and a child process that trusts it, showing the socket goes to the pinned address with one lookup per request and a certificate for another name is refused (1 mutation checked: the pinned lookup removed fails it and three older tests). Tests 455 to 457.
- **G6. README:** rewritten for a first-time visitor: a one-paragraph pitch, a 30-second install from source (and the `--omit=optional` install for v1.0), sample output trimmed from a real run (a page with a hidden instruction, then a question and its passage), what it does by capability rather than by version, an honest comparison with Playwright MCP, Fetch MCP, Browser Use and Jina Reader, and the user agent written from the version. The reference sections that follow lost their "(v0.x branch, unreleased)" labels and now describe v0.5.1 (ports, redirects, the request timer, exit codes, MCP annotations). The README's principles list was replaced by a pointer to section 2 here.
- **Round 69 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-69.md`: HIGH 2, MEDIUM 4, LOW 1), the two HIGH fixed with a test that fails without each fix (2 mutations checked):** (1) the wall rule counted invisible source text: script bodies, `<head>`, and hidden subtrees (a `<main hidden>` after the form) could pad a login page past the thresholds. The counting now runs on `visibleHtml()`, the markup with comments, `<script>`, `<style>`, `<noscript>`, `<template>`, `<head>` and every hidden element's subtree (`hidden`, `aria-hidden="true"`, inline `display:none` or `visibility:hidden`) removed, one bounded `indexOf` pass. (2) Pruning `slips/revoked.jsonl` could rename an older copy over a revoke appended meanwhile, so a copy of that slip put back would be used again. Appending and pruning now take the same lock (`lockfile.ts`, shared with the spacing file); a revoke that cannot take it within a second fails loudly before anything changes, and a prune that cannot is skipped. MEDIUM and LOW findings are carried (see Known issues). Tests 457 to 459.
- **Round 70 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-70.md`: HIGH 2, MEDIUM 0, LOW 0), both fixed with a test that fails without each fix (2 mutations checked):** (1) the round 69 wall fix still matched markup as text: `opacity:0`, `font-size:0` or page-style-hidden padding counted as visible, and `title=" hidden "` on a login form was read as the `hidden` attribute and removed the form. `visibleHtml()` now PARSES the page (htmlparser2, as the reader does) and drops every element the reader's own `hiddenReason()` hides (with the page's `<style>` rules), then writes back minimal markup (each visible element with only its type, class, id and role; text with `<` and `>` turned into spaces) for the same checks. (2) a revocation line torn by a crash could swallow the next revoke's line, so its slip, put back, was used again. Each revocation record now starts with a newline, is written whole (looping on short writes) and fsynced before the slip is retired. Tests 459 to 461.
- **Round 71 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-71.md`: HIGH 4, MEDIUM 0, LOW 0), the last round the budget allows. Not fixed: the release stops here for the coordinator.** (1) `visibleHtml()` keeps the text inside `<iframe>` and `<canvas>`, which the reader skips, so 900 characters of fallback content spare a login page (fix: share the reader's skip set). (2) the page is lowercased before it is parsed, so a page-style rule `.hide` hides a form whose class is `HIDE` in the wall check though it is visible (fix: parse the original HTML; lowercase only the written-back markup). (3) the traversal stops at 200,000 steps (closing tags counted) and returns partial markup, so a password form after 100,000 open `<p>` tags is missed (fix: throw `ComplexityError` when the budget runs out with work left). (4) `pruneRevoked` writes the new list with one unchecked `writeSync`; a short write (disk full) renames a truncated list over the good one and drops live revocations (fix: loop on short writes, and keep the old list on any failure).

- **Round 71 HIGH and round 69 MEDIUM/LOW fixed (Travis's rule, 2026-10-22: nothing is carried), each with a test that fails without its fix (9 checks: 8 mutations and the never-rewritten test):** walls: the wall check uses the reader's own list of unrendered elements (`NOT_RENDERED` in `shield.ts`, now one list: iframe, canvas, object, svg, select and the rest), parses the page in its original case (only the written-back markup is lowercased), and fails closed (`page_too_complex`) when its node budget runs out with work left. Revocations: the list is never rewritten (the prune and its lock were removed: every way of rewriting it could lose a live line); it is capped at 8 MB (about 80,000 revocations) and fails loudly past that. Tickets: a claim's own time is in its name (`.claimed-<id>.<ms>.<random>`), since a rename keeps the ticket's old mtime. Reader: `<pre>` text is gathered under the walk's rules (unrendered and hidden parts left out and flagged, `<br>` a line break); main-content scoring never counts hidden, unrendered or boilerplate subtrees. Spacing: the shared file keeps the last start too, and a reservation waits for both the last reservation's next slot and its own spacing after the last start. Disk cap: never evicts a `.tmp` younger than a day. Tests 461 to 467.
- **Round 72 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-72.md`: HIGH 7, MEDIUM 1, reconstructed from Codex's reproductions because its answer was cut by OpenAI's content filter), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (9 mutations checked):** one page style sheet for the reader and the wall check (`pageSheet` in `shield.ts`): a `<style>` inside `<template>` or `<noscript>` is inert, one with a media other than all or screen does not apply, rules are read in order so a later `display:block` undoes an earlier `display:none` for the same class or id, and an inline style setting the same property to a visible value wins. Walls: "content after" means a `<main>` or `<article>` element (exact name) whose own text reaches 1,200 visible characters; an `<iframe>`, `<object>` or `<embed>` is kept as a marker tag with its address (a CAPTCHA served in a frame is seen), never its fallback text. Deflate: zlib or raw is decided by trying the first 64 KB as zlib (output capped), not by the header bytes. Revocations: each record is one write; a short write fails the revoke before the slip is retired. Tests 467 to 470.
- **Round 73 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-73.md`: HIGH 5, MEDIUM 3, LOW 2), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (11 mutations checked):** walls: a CSS judgement can no longer hide a wall's MARKERS: an element a style seems to hide keeps its tags (a password box, a CAPTCHA widget or frame), only its text is dropped, so a misjudged rule can only make a page look thinner (a false wall, never a missed one); only letters and digits count as content (no-break and zero-width spaces padded a page); the whole fetched page is parsed, not a 512 KB prefix (the node budget fails closed); `<embed>` and `<frame>` are markers like `<iframe>`. Style rules: each property is decided across all of an element's classes and its id (`!important`, then an id over a class, then the later rule), an inline value beats a rule that is not `!important`; `opacity` hides only for a whole CSS number equal to 0, and `clip: rect()` only for an empty rectangle. Deflate: the body is collected (to the cap) and decided by inflating all of it as zlib, else raw; then streamed through the chosen decoder. Politeness: a call holds its site's turn until the answer has been read (a call holds one site at a time: a hop to another site gives the old turn back, so two calls never wait on each other). `<pre>` has no cut of its own. A watch fingerprint lives in its own file (`<name>.fp.json`), so writing it can never bring back a removed bookmark. `bookmark` exits 2 on a refusal. Tests 470 to 475.
- **Round 74 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-74.md`: HIGH 5, MEDIUM 3, LOW 1), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (9 mutations checked):** a declaration block is read as the cascade reads it (`!important` beats a normal declaration of the same property, else the later one wins; `parseDecls`); `opacity` accepts the whole CSS number grammar, exponents included; default-ignorable characters (fillers such as U+3164 that are letters but draw nothing) are dropped before letters are counted; CAPTCHA marker words are taken from the WHOLE class and id before the written-back attribute is shortened; the bot-check title is also read from the parsed document, not only a raw prefix; robots.txt holds its site's turn until it has been read (`Turns` in `queue.ts`, shared with pages and probes; a hop to another site gives the old turn back before anything else); a same-site redirect still reserves the shared slot and rechecks the cooldown right before sending; the deflate cap is on decoded bytes (the compressed input gets room for deflate's framing); a failed duplicate `bookmark add` keeps the existing watch baseline. Tests 475 to 478.
- **Round 75 (`reviews/2026-10-22-codex-round-75.md`: HIGH 7, MEDIUM 5, LOW 1), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (14 mutations checked):** the wall count is now an ALLOW-LIST (`cssTaint` and `plain` in `shield.ts`): text counts only inside elements no style could plausibly hide (no hidden or aria-hidden attribute, no inline style declaring anything outside a short list of safe properties, no class or id named by a page rule that declares anything outside that list); a risky rule with any other kind of selector (an element, `*`, an attribute, a pseudo-class), or more page CSS than is read, means no text counts. This ends the run of CSS findings (invalid declarations, units, percentages, exponents, negative values, the cascade, read limits): the wall check no longer judges CSS at all. Text in `nav`, `footer`, `header` and `aside` never counts, judged by parsed ancestry. Markers are found everywhere: the title by its own walk (in `<head>` too), frame addresses read whole before they are shortened, every form and every password box with no search caps (single forward passes). Politeness: every hop gives its turn back and queues again (a robots refresh during a same-site redirect cannot wait on itself), and a held turn is also held across processes (an `.active` file beside the spacing file, broken when its process is gone or after 75 s); without the spacing file's lock nothing is written. Reader: grouped JSON-LD blocks are exact slices of the text; `<pre>` trimming is linear. A watch fingerprint is bound to the bookmark it was taken for (`added_at`), so it never attaches to a bookmark added again under the same name. Tests 478 to 484.
- **Round 76 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-76.md`: HIGH 6, MEDIUM 6, LOW 1).** By the coordinator's decision of 2026-10-23 (section 9), findings 1 and 2 (padding that hides a site's own login wall from the count) and 10 and 11 (CSS that keeps hidden text from being flagged) are by design: walls and hidden-text flags are advisory. The rest are fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (6 mutations checked): a challenge served in a frame (Turnstile, reCAPTCHA) is a marker on its own, form or not; an `<object>`'s address is its `data`; the page's main and article elements and a prefix count of visible characters are computed once (`layout`), so no per-marker search, no cap that fails open, and no quadratic scan; private calls and previews ignore a stale cross-process lease and never remove it; a stale lease is broken only under the per-site lock; the lease is taken before the shared slot is reserved; links inside `<pre>` are collected with their `[n]` markers; block elements inside a table cell no longer split the row. The Codex prompt from round 77 states the threat model. Tests 484 to 487.
- **Round 77 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-77.md`: HIGH 1, MEDIUM 2, LOW 1), all fixed with tests (3 mutations checked; the writer guard on stale recovery is defence in depth for a race window and is not isolable by a test, since the read-only path returns before it):** only a writer ever recovers a stale lease (a private call never takes the site lock or removes a file); a writer makes `hosts/` before taking its lease, so the first request to a site holds one too; the per-site queues and waiting sets are no longer LRU-evicted (an evicted live queue let a second request in), they hold only sites in use and empty out as they drain; JSON-LD is printed one value per line before it is split, so a minified block diffs by line too (a structured-data question now finds the matching line among the top passages; one older test asks for that). Tests 487 to 488.
- **Round 78 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-78.md`: HIGH 0, MEDIUM 3, LOW 4), all fixed with tests (6 mutations checked; the bounded JSON-LD printer is covered by a test that would run out of memory without it):** JSON-LD is printed with the bounded printer under the remaining structured-data budget, keeping the raw text when the print does not fit; a site's queue is forgotten only once its last turn has really settled (a cancelled waiter no longer drops a queue in use); `lastHit` keeps a site while a spacing can still be owed (a minute) and prunes by age, not by count; a list item's marker leads its first content (no lone bullet before paragraphs); `start="0"` is kept; a `|` inside a table cell is escaped; a `<pre>` fence is longer than any run of backticks inside it. Tests 488 to 490.
- **Round 79 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-79.md`: HIGH 1, MEDIUM 1, LOW 5), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (8 mutations checked):** HIGH: a form holding a CAPTCHA embedded as an `<object>` (its address in `data`) was not refused, so a slip could have sent it; `data` is now one of the attributes the form CAPTCHA guard reads. The queue remembers the earliest time each site's NEXT request may start, so a 30-second Crawl-delay holds even when the next request (a private one, which writes no shared file) asks for 1 second. Reader: a list item's later blocks are indented under its marker and a `<pre>` item keeps its marker; signed start numbers, `reversed` lists and an item's own `value` are honoured; a table inside a cell no longer adds outer columns; search splits Chinese, Japanese, Korean and Thai by script extensions, so words with the long-vowel mark are found. Tests 490 to 492.
- **Round 80 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-80.md`: HIGH 1, MEDIUM 1, LOW 4), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (6 mutations checked):** HIGH: a form holding a Cloudflare challenge (an `<object>` or frame on `challenges.cloudflare.com`, without the words captcha or turnstile) was not refused; the form guard and the wall check now share one predicate (`challengeText` in `forms.ts`). The CSS number pattern no longer backtracks quadratically. A `<pre>` inside a list item keeps the item's indentation (continuation or nesting); continuation is indented under the marker's own width (`100. `). Search splits each word into runs of no-space scripts and the rest, so `サーバー2台` still finds `サーバー`. The MCP `receipts` reply counts its receipts. Tests 492 to 493. No round 81 was run: round 80 is the practical guard's limit.
- **Round 81 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-81.md`: HIGH 1, MEDIUM 1, LOW 4), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (6 mutations checked):** HIGH: a challenge frame whose address was percent-encoded still slipped past the form guard. This is the third form of the same finding (rounds 79 and 80 each taught the guard one more way to read an address), so the guard is now an ALLOW-LIST: a form that embeds any `<iframe>`, `<frame>`, `<object>` or `<embed>` is `not_allowed` (`captcha`), whatever its address says; and a form too large to check whole (node budget) is `not_allowed` (`too_large`) instead of passing unchecked. A no-store robots.txt fetched by one call is not shared with calls waiting on it (each fetches its own). Every line of a `<pre>` inside a list item, fences included, is indented under the item; a nested list starts under its parent item's text, whatever the parent marker's width; search indexes single characters of Chinese, Japanese, Korean and Thai too, so a one-character query finds its passage; an unknown CLI command is one JSON error line. Tests 493 to 495.
- **Round 82 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-82.md`: HIGH 2, MEDIUM 0, LOW 1), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (3 mutations checked):** HIGH: the round 81 allow-list ran inside the field walk, which skips what is inside a `<button>`, so a frame there was not seen; the refusal markers (an embedded frame, a CAPTCHA marker, a password or file input) are now looked for on a walk of their own over the WHOLE form that skips nothing, and a form too large for it is refused. HIGH: a deflate body over the compressed-input budget was cut and decoded in part for a caller that takes only the first bytes, so a robots.txt could lose a Disallow; it is now refused whole (robots.txt then fails closed). A long block in a list item keeps the item's indent on every piece it is split into. Tests 495 to 496.
- **Round 83 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-83.md`: HIGH 0, MEDIUM 2, LOW 4), all fixed with tests (5 mutations checked; judging a declaration once per rule is covered by a timing test):** a style declaration is judged once per rule, not once per selector; a Crawl-delay learned from robots.txt applies from then on (`noteDelay`), even when the asked path is denied and no page request follows; a fresh 200 with identical bytes takes the site's current type and `door_detail`; a heading that takes a pending list marker leads its item; an item that starts with a nested list keeps its own marker (written as its own line); a backslash in a table cell is escaped before its pipes. Tests 496 to 497.
- **Round 84 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-84.md`: HIGH 0, MEDIUM 3, LOW 0), all fixed with tests (1 mutation checked; the lease file's type check is backed by `ownFile`, which also refuses a non-regular file, so dropping either alone is not isolable):** a Crawl-delay learned from robots.txt moves the shared spacing file's next slot too (`extendNext`, under the lock, never in private mode or a preview), so other processes keep it; the question's length counts when choosing inline or worker search; the lease file is read like every store file (no links, no blocking, a regular file of ours, at most 256 bytes; anything else at its name is treated as stale and removed under the lock). Tests 497 to 498.
- **Round 85 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-85.md`: HIGH 1), fixed with a test that fails without it (1 mutation checked):** the round 82 whole-form scan had its own, shorter list of refusal markers, so a card-number or one-time-code input inside a `<button>` (which the field walk skips) did not refuse the form. There is now ONE per-element refusal check (`elementRefusals`: CAPTCHA markers and embedded frames, password and file inputs, login and card `autocomplete` tokens, payment-looking names and values), used by both the whole-form scan, which visits every element, and the field walk. Tests 498 to 499.
- **Round 86 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-86.md`: HIGH 1, MEDIUM 1), both fixed afterwards with tests that fail without the fix (2 mutations checked):** HIGH: a card-number input inside a `<button>`, flagged only by its `<label>` or `title`, still escaped the refusals (the field walk does not enter a button, and label checks live there). This is the third form of the same finding (rounds 82 and 85), so it is fixed by an ALLOW-LIST: a form control inside any container the field walk does not enter (a button, a select, a textarea, a template, a script or style) refuses the form outright, with a new code `nested_control`. MEDIUM: robots.txt now holds its site's turn until it has been read, parsed and its Crawl-delay noted, so a request queued behind it waits the learned delay. Tests 499 to 500. No round 87 was run: round 86 was the guard's limit and the same finding had survived two fixes.
- **Round 87 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-87.md`: HIGH 4, LOW 1; the form guard reviewed as one design), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (7 mutations checked):** the form guard is now ONE audit (`auditForm`) under one rule: refuse unless every control is a plain, top-level, recognized field. It walks every element of the form, skipping nothing; refuses a form found inside a container the field walk does not enter (`nested_control`, tracked from the page walk), any control nested in one, and any input type Tiresias does not know (`unknown_control`, new; it was read as text); judges EVERY control, hidden, disabled, unnamed and buttons included, on its names, title, placeholder, value and its COMPLETE labels (the `for` label and every enclosing label, never the shortened display text), and judges every legend, option (text and value) and textarea content, for card numbers and payment words, login words (password, passcode, login code, one-time, verification code and similar) and CAPTCHA words; checks the form element itself; and refuses (`too_large`) when any one text is over 64 KB, all texts over 1 MB, or the walk over its node budget. Hidden values are masked in object keys too (a site's header name). An `error` reply from the CLI is one JSON line on standard error. Tests 500 to 502.
- **Round 88 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-88.md`: HIGH 2, LOW 1), all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix (6 mutations checked):** HIGH: a control's meaning could still sit where the audit did not tie it to the control (external labels, ARIA references, a datalist, an optgroup's label, an ancestor's title, a button's caption, text split across inline elements, full-width letters). The audit no longer ties texts to controls at all: it reads EVERYTHING the form says (every text node, both with and without spaces between them, and every attribute value of every element), every element outside the form that its controls point to (`for` labels, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `list`) with their attributes, and its ancestors' titles, all NFKC-normalized, and refuses on any payment, login or CAPTCHA marker. HIGH: header names arrive lowercased, so hidden values are masked in keys against lowercased secrets too. LOW: every CLI reply with status `error` (bookmark, safety and others) is one JSON line on standard error. Tests 502 to 504.
- **Round 89 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-89.md`: HIGH 2), both fixed:** HIGH (limits): over 50 ids in one reference attribute, or an ancestor title too long, now refuse instead of being skipped (2 mutations checked). HIGH (meaning): the same form-audit finding for the third round, so the guard stopped work; Travis chose the structural rewrite (option a). `auditForm` is now one bounded worklist over the form, everything any element points to (all labels, five ARIA reference kinds, list), wrapping labels, enclosing legends and ancestors, with the same checks on every element and each unit's text judged with and without inline joins; the field walk's overlapping checks were deleted, so one path remains. Tests for every round 87 to 89 case (14 mutations checked, all caught). Tests 504 to 506.
- **Round 90 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-90.md`: HIGH 4, the form guard reviewed as one design), all fixed after Travis chose (a), each with a test that fails without it (15 mutations checked, all caught):** meaning split across a control's name, labels, references and context is checked as one bag of words for the many-word markers (the round-89 rewrite had lost the field walk's name-plus-label check); id lists split on ASCII whitespace only and `list` is one exact id; script and style elements are in the id index; invisible and format characters are removed before matching. Tests 506 to 507.
- **Round 91 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-91.md`: HIGH 2, MEDIUM 1, LOW 1), all fixed after Travis chose (a), each with a test that fails without it:** the form guard keeps one word set for the whole form, fed by every text the audit reads, checked against one marker list where phrases also match run together (HIGH 1 and 2); each element is visited once, so overlapping units cost nothing extra (MEDIUM); a `<pre>` no longer counts link markers as nodes, and past the real node limit the page is refused as too complex instead of being cut without notice (LOW). The per-control mix and the old PAY, LOGIN_WORDS and CAPTCHA_WORDS lists are deleted: one path, one list. Tests 507 to 509.
- **Round 92 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-92.md`: HIGH 2), both fixed with tests that fail without them (6 mutations checked):** the audit finds every covered root first, then walks only the outermost roots' whole subtrees, so a reference that reaches part of the form first can no longer make a later walk skip a joined word or a nested_control check; "not robot" is back, and a test loops over every phrase of the pre-merge lists and round 90's word sets. The dead `<pre>` backstop is deleted. Tests 509 to 510.
- **Round 93 (`reviews/2026-10-23-codex-round-93.md`: HIGH 2, LOW 1), all fixed, each with a test that fails without it:** one normalization path (NFKC, case fold, invisible characters out) with unsplit, camelCase-split and run-together tokens for every text, covered by a table test of case mixes, fullwidth letters and digits, ligatures, the long s and sharp s, and digits split by spaces, no-break spaces, dashes and zero-width characters; card digits checked per attribute value and per text node; `<pre>` boilerplate text left out with its links kept, and the `<pre>` backstop restored. Tests 510 to 512.
- **Round 94 (`reviews/2026-10-24-codex-round-94.md`: HIGH 2), both fixed with tests that fail without them:** invisible characters are removed before capitals are looked at; every marker is searched inside every run-together chunk (the separate CAPTCHA-inside list folded into it), with the many-word markers still matched apart (camelCase-split words only: the unsplit word variant added nothing and is deleted). A test pins the ordinary words now refused. Two older tests (rounds 51 and 52) use "%2fpanel" instead of "%2fadmin", which now also reads "2fa" and is refused for that reason first. Tests 512 to 514.
- **Round 95 (`reviews/2026-10-24-codex-round-95.md`: HIGH 2), both fixed with tests that fail without them:** long markers are found inside skeletons (letters and digits only, per attribute value and per block of inline-joined text) by the one MARKERS check, short ones (under 5 characters) inside chunks; the whole-word variant is back for the words of many-word markers that sit apart, and long such words also count inside skeletons; card digits are checked on each inline-joined block too. A test pins every round-94 and round-95 phrase, the 5-character threshold, and the grown list of ordinary words now refused. Tests 514 to 515.
- **Round 96 (`reviews/2026-10-24-codex-round-96.md`: HIGH 1, LOW 1), both fixed with tests that fail without them (5 mutations checked, all caught):** blocks are kept as an array during the walk and white space inside a block is collapsed to spaces, so a line break, CRLF or tab inside "password", "captcha", "routing" or a card number no longer splits it; each element's attributes are judged once, so a long harmless title on a wrapping label no longer reaches too_large. Tests 515 to 516.
- **Round 97 (`reviews/2026-10-24-codex-round-97.md`: LOW 2; no form-guard finding), both fixed with tests that fail without them (3 mutations checked, all caught):** search pairs for no-space scripts are built from code points, so a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane no longer makes half-pair tokens; a CLI error reply goes to standard error whether or not `--text` is set (the stream follows the status, the format follows `--text`). Tests 516 to 517.

**Not in v0.5.1:** W-24 to W-28 (registered 2026-10-20, targeted v0.6 to v0.8), everything in the v0.6 "inside files" scope.

### v0.5.0 (2026-10-20)

Released: dangerous files (W-20) and the owner's own scanner (W-21), built to the coordinator's scope in section 4.4, after Codex round 68, the final, severity-rated round, found nothing HIGH (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-68.md`: 0 high, 1 medium, 1 low). The entries below are the work log kept while v0.5 was built.

**Known issues carried to v0.5.1** (from round 68, not fixed in v0.5; first planned for v0.6, moved to v0.5.1 on 2026-10-20):

- MEDIUM: a body that starts with `#!` is named a script before the disk-image trailer is looked at, so a text body with a `#!` first line and a `koly` trailer is read as script text (flag `script`) instead of a file warning; fetched, saved and MCP text alike. Nothing runs either way; the fix is to give the trailer precedence in `signature()`.
- LOW: `clear` and the daily trim plan the disk cap before sweeping stale quarantine copies, so the cap can evict cache files the sweep alone would have made room for, and the bytes the sweep frees are missing from `total_bytes_freed` and `store_bytes_after`.

- **Added (W-20):** `files.ts`, a fixed first-bytes signature table (programs, scripts' `#!`, shortcuts, add-ons, archives and disk images including ISO 9660 at its offset and the dmg `koly` trailer, old Office, PDF, images, audio and video) and the declared type and name rules. Every response is named from its first 40 KB in `safety.ts` (`readBody`, replacing `readCapped`): a page is kept as before; a file is hashed (SHA-256) as it streams, never held whole and never written, up to `TIRESIAS_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (50 MB); a `Content-Length` over it is not downloaded. New `status: "file_warning"` with a top-level `file_warning` replaces "That content type is not supported", from read, check, watch, resume (retry, saved file, MCP text) and a form's answer. Scripts, CSV, calendar and contact files are read as plain text with their flags (`script`, `csv_formulas` with `counts.csv_formulas`, `calendar`, `contact`), SVG as element text with `svg`, shortcuts served as text are file warnings, and PDFs gain `pdf_javascript`, `pdf_attachments` and `pdf_launch`. `type_mismatch` and `double_extension` flags. History lines gain `file_kind` and `scan`; the receipts day file gains `file: "warning"` records; the daily log, the needs report (`counts.files`) and `watch` (`attention` outcome `file_warning`, `counts.file_warnings`) count them.
- **Added (W-21):** `scanner.ts`. `tiresias scanner set [--timeout N] [--send-file] -- <full path> [args...]`, `scanner show`, `scanner off`; `config.json` key `scanner`. The hook runs with no shell in its own process group, gets one JSON object on standard input, and its at most 4 KB of output is parsed strictly; `file_warning.scan: { verdict, by }`, the note in `untrusted_content.scan_note`. `send_file` writes a quarantine copy (0600, random name) as the file streams, deleted after the hook and again at the end of the call; never in private mode. `clear` and the daily trim delete copies older than 10 minutes (`quarantine` in the report).
- **Changed:** `PARSER_VERSION` is 9. A cached page at a URL that now serves a file is removed. A missing type with a `.html`, `.json` or `.txt` name is read as that. MCP descriptions of read, check, watch, resume and form_submit say what to do with a file. Builder's decisions are the two 2026-10-20 rows in section 9.
- **Round 58 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-58.md`, 8 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (10 mutations checked):** page-only fetches (robots.txt, probes, doors, a form page) keep a file's body as text as before, so a robots.txt starting `MZ` is parsed, not read as no rules (the "stop at the first bytes" path was removed); a name that asks for a stricter rule (shortcut, script, CSV, SVG, calendar, contact) wins over the declared type; a charset claim no longer exempts a body from the binary check, and UTF-16 bodies are decoded by their byte-order mark for every content check (shortcut marks included); a file a person saved obeys the file cap; a form answer with any 2xx is hashed; hooks start through a runner that kills the hook's process group when Tiresias is gone or the cap runs out; the storage-limit loader ignores the scanner key, so a bad scanner setting stops neither resume nor `clear`. Tests are 400.
- **Round 59 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-59.md`, 5 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (8 mutations checked):** the text rules are collected and the strictest wins (shortcut, CSV, script, SVG, calendar, contact, then the declared type), and a shortcut mark is looked for after a `#!` line; a cached copy's fingerprint covers how it was read, so the same bytes under a new kind are not served with the old flags; a file a person saved is classified from its first bytes and hashed piece by piece (never read whole, not held to the page cap); the scanner is never started on Windows; `counts.csv_formulas` is on every reply that carries the flag, form answers included. Tests are 405.
- **Round 60 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-60.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with one test that fails without each fix (3 mutations checked):** a file a person saved is decoded exactly like a fetched body with no type (a UTF-8 or UTF-16 byte-order mark decides, then a `<meta>` charset), so a UTF-16 CSV keeps its formula count and a UTF-16 login page is still a wall; a `Content-Length` of any length (leading zeroes included) is compared with the file cap; a file attached to a PDF page (a `FileAttachment` annotation, not only the catalog's list) gives `pdf_attachments`, from annotation metadata only. Tests are 406.
- **Round 61 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-61.md`, 4 blocking), all fixed with a test that fails without each fix (4 mutations checked):** a declared name over 255 characters keeps its END (where the extensions are), so a long `....mobileconfig` is still a shortcut; PDF attachment annotations are looked for with every intent, so a hidden (`NoView`) one is flagged; CSV records end at LF, CR or CRLF, so a CR-only file has its formulas counted; a file that stalls before 40 KB arrived is still named from what did arrive and comes back as a warning without a fingerprint. Tests are 407.
- **Round 62 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-62.md`, 2 blocking), both fixed with a test that fails without each fix (2 mutations checked):** the whole `Content-Disposition` header is read (an 8 KB cut-off had let a padded header hide a `.mobileconfig` name; Node already bounds header size); the PDF `/Launch` scan ends a name at PDF's own white-space and delimiter bytes, listed explicitly, NUL included. Tests are 408.
- **Round 63 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-63.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (4 mutations checked):** a response declared HTML is checked for wall markers whatever reader its name chose (a login page at `/login.csv` is a wall), and so is a saved page that starts with markup; the hook runner now keeps the hook's output itself, answers Tiresias in one line, and then kills its whole group with SIGKILL, staying alive (with its time cap and parent watchdog) until then, so a helper that ignores SIGTERM cannot outlive it; a body whose last 512 bytes start with the disk-image trailer `koly` is a file (kind `archive`) even when it arrived looking like text, fetched or saved. Not isolable by a test: the runner's own kill right after it answers, because Tiresias kills the group the moment it reads the answer. Tests are 410.
