- **Round 64 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-64.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (4 mutations checked):** the wall check of a response declared HTML uses the visible-text length the HTML reader finds, whatever reader was chosen, so markup padding cannot hide a login page; a body named a disk image by its trailer is held to the file cap like any file (over it: no fingerprint, no copy), and a saved file's trailer is read on its own (the last 512 bytes) before anything else; `Content-Disposition` is split by a small linear tokenizer that honours quotes and takes only the exact keys `filename*` and `filename`, and when the two names differ both are kept and the strictest rule of either applies. Tests are 411.
- **Round 65 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-65.md`, 4 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (5 mutations checked):** for a read, a page candidate is hashed and its last 512 bytes kept as it arrives and it is held to the smaller of the page cap and the file cap, so a disk image named only by its trailer needs no second pass and its copy is written in pieces; every distinct name `Content-Disposition` gives counts (repeated parameters included); `doors` parses a response declared HTML as HTML whatever its name, so it sees its walls; the hook runner is given Tiresias's pid and stops itself before starting the hook when its parent is not that process. Tests are 412.
- **Round 66 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-66.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (4 mutations checked):** the first bytes looked at are never more than the file cap allows (a 1 KB cap stops at 1 KB, for downloads and saved files alike); ONE rule decides whether a response is HTML for the wall check, whatever reader its name chose: declared HTML, or page markup (`<html`, `<body`, `<form`, a doctype) in its first 64 KB, used by reads, `doors`, form answers and saved pages; a `filename*` in ISO-8859-1 is decoded as such, and one in another charset, or that does not decode, is kept as written, so its extension still counts (a name is never dropped). Tests are 413.
- **Round 67 (`reviews/2026-10-20-codex-round-67.md`, 3 blocking).** Round 67 was the tenth round, the first cap; the coordinator then decided the fixes below and allowed rounds 68 to 70. Found: (1) a saved page (file or MCP text) classified as text from a head shortened by a small file cap is then read whole under only the fetch cap, so bytes past the head (a NUL) escape the binary check; (2) SVG elements with a namespace prefix (`<s:script>`, `<s:style>`) are not dropped, because the reader compares literal tag names; (3) a body named a disk image by its trailer keeps the flags of its text classification, so `type_mismatch` is missing (fetched and saved alike).
- **Round 67 fixes (coordinator's decisions, 2026-10-20), each with a test that fails without it (6 mutations checked):** a saved page (file or MCP text) is classified again WHOLE before it is read, so binary content anywhere makes it a file; SVG elements whose local name is `script`, `style` or `foreignObject` are removed first, whatever their namespace prefix or case; the disk-image trailer is one more signature on the same path (`classify` takes the last 512 bytes when known), so a file found by it gets its flags from the signature like every other file, and the separate trailer branches were removed.
- **Fixed (slips test flake):** slip ids and handoff ticket ids are drawn from letters, digits and `_` (63 symbols, no bias; 24 and 16 characters, about 143 and 96 bits), so an id never starts with `-` and is never taken for a command-line option. Old ids keep working. Tests are 415.
- **Tests:** 395 (20 new in `v05.test.ts`; every file is a few bytes made by the test, served locally; the only commands run are the test's own node scripts as the owner's scanner). Each guard was checked to fail its test with the guard reverted (32 mutations: signature over the declared type, page-only fetches stopping at the head, the streaming cap, the Content-Length cap, the timeout warning, shortcut by type and by name, scripts and SVG not parsed as markdown, the double-extension inner check, the CSV quoted-cell start, calendar unfolding, the dmg trailer, the three PDF flags, dropping the cached copy, no record in private mode, no shell, the hook timeout, the 4 KB cap, non-zero exit, the verdict allow-list, the note type, no hook for low risk, the note inside untrusted_content, the untrusted marker, no quarantine in private mode, mode 0600, a bad setting reported, the leftover age, and the call-end quarantine deletion). Not isolable by a test: the deletion right after the hook, because the call-end deletion is its backup and runs moments later. A route-extension list was found to be dead code by its mutation and removed.

### v0.4.0 (2026-10-18)

Released: profiles (W-18), permission slips (W-05) and permission-gated forms (W-04), after Codex round 57 found nothing blocking. The entries below are the work log kept while v0.4 was built.

Phase C, built on the `v0.4-forms` branch (on top of phases A and B), not yet reviewed by Codex:

- **Added (W-04):** forms as permission-gated actions. HTML reads list `untrusted_content.forms` (fields, labels, options; hidden fields by name only) and `counts.forms`; forms with a password, a file upload, payment fields, a CAPTCHA or anything Tiresias cannot send exactly carry a fixed `not_allowed` code. MCP `form_preview` and `form_submit` (the server now lists fifteen tools) and CLI `tiresias form preview|submit`. A preview fetches the page fresh and shows the exact request with hidden values as `[set by the site]`, a `preview_hash` and whether a slip covers the target, and writes nothing. A submit re-fetches, refuses a changed form (`form_changed`), checks the target, claims one slip use, sends once (301/302/303 as GET, 307/308 not followed) with only the cookies the site set during that call, and reads the answer like `read`. Every request that left is recorded in the receipts log (`sent: "form"`). Refused in private mode.
- **Changed:** `PARSER_VERSION` is 8 (cached pages are read again and gain their forms). `safeFetch` can send a method and body on the first hop, with the submit redirect rule and a per-call cookie source. History lines of tool `form_submit` carry `slip`. The daily log counts forms sent. `fitEnvelope` can drop `forms`.
- **Round 51 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-51.md`, 5 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (9 mutations checked), by the coordinator's decisions (remove moving parts, no masking machinery):** a GET form with any hidden field is refused (`hidden_in_url`), so no hidden value can reach a URL, reply or record; a GET submit follows no redirect (a 302 to itself had repeated it six times); a form action or slip-matched URL with `%2f`, `%5c`, `%2e`, a backslash or a dot segment is refused (`unusual_path`, in the form and in `checkSlip`/`claimSlip`); payment detection covers every field, hidden ones included, and card-number values (Luhn); preview never downloads or writes the Safe Browsing list (read-only, like private mode). Tests are 363.
- **Round 52 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-52.md`, 3 blocking, 1 non-blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (6 mutations checked), by the coordinator's decisions (remove moving parts):** a submit follows no redirect at all, POST included (a POST to 302 loop had sent one POST and five GETs; the redirect path for submits was deleted); a submit target's or slip-matched URL's path may hold no `%` at all, as written or after parsing (`unusual_path`; double and triple encodings, `%20` and parser-encoded letters included; a known cost); a card number anywhere in the final request, from an option, a default, a hidden value or the agent's text, is refused as `payment`, checked before the hash and again on the encoded request before the slip is claimed (the second check reads the same values back from the bytes, so no test can tell it apart from the first); a visible field sharing a hidden field's name is `duplicate_names`. Tests are 367.
- **Round 53 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-53.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (8 mutations checked), by the coordinator's decision to stop deny-listing and use allow-lists so the class goes away:** paths are allow-listed (form actions as written and resolved, slip matching, slip grants' `--path`), replacing the round 51 and 52 deny-lists, which are deleted; `/contact/..;/admin` is `unusual_path`. Field names are allow-listed (`bad_field_name`), so `role&#10;x` and `role&#13;x` can no longer collide after encoding. The card-number check covers the whole final request, URL path and query included, so `/contact/pay?ref=4111111111111111` is `payment`. Not isolable by a test: the slip-side check of the parsed path (for an absolute URL the written-path rule already implies it) and the pre-claim card check (the same function on the same bytes as the earlier one); both are kept as backups because the rule says both must pass. A `/x/../contact` URL no longer matches a `/contact` slip (the W-05 matching test was updated). Tests are 370.
- **Round 54 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-54.md`, 3 blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (5 mutations checked), by the coordinator's decisions:** names are compared in canonical form for collisions (`role_flag` and `role.flag` are `duplicate_names`), also against a POST target's own query names; a POST form with no action of its own on a page address with a query is `query_on_page` (Codex's `/contact?%72ole=admin`); hidden values of 6 or more characters echoed by the site after a submit are masked as `[hidden value]` everywhere in the reply, and receipts that would quote one are withheld (`counts.receipts_withheld`), in the reply and the stored log. A new section 9 row states what form submission guarantees, and what it leaves to the site. Tests are 373.
- **Round 55 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-55.md`, 1 blocking, under guarantee (e)), fixed with a test that fails without it (1 mutation checked):** the echo masks are built from each hidden value as written and as sent (CRLF line breaks), raw, form-urlencoded and percent-encoded, so a hidden `secret&#10;abcdef` echoed as `secret%0D%0Aabcdef` in a Location is masked. Tests are 374.
- **Round 56 (`reviews/2026-10-18-codex-round-56.md`, 1 blocking, under guarantee (e)), fixed with tests that fail without their fix (3 mutations checked), by the coordinator's decision to use an allow-list instead of more mask variants:** a hidden value must be printable ASCII with no whitespace (`bad_hidden_value`), so Codex's `secret&#10;abcdef`, which reached a reply as `secret abcdef` after HTML whitespace collapsing, is refused and nothing is sent; round 55's CRLF variants are removed as unreachable; masks are the raw, form-urlencoded, percent-encoded and HTML-escaped spellings (a token with `+ / = & < > ~ ! ( )` is masked in a Location, in page text and kept out of stored receipts). The HTML-escaped mask cannot be told apart by a test, since raw HTML is never returned; it is kept in case it ever is. Tests are 375.
- **Tests:** 358 (14 new in `forms.test.ts`; every form is sent to a local test server). Each guard was checked to fail its test with the fix reverted (18 mutations: the hidden value in the listing and in the preview, the hash check, claiming before the target checks, following 307, cookies to another host, private mode, a GET without a slip, the Safe Browsing check of the target before the claim, sending a not-allowed form, preview writing history, the password, payment and CAPTCHA refusals, a hidden value from the agent, required fields, a jar shared between calls, and a hidden value in the record).

Phase B, built on the `v0.4-slips` branch (on top of phase A), not yet reviewed by Codex:

- **Added (W-05):** permission slips. `tiresias slip grant --action submit_form --site <origin> [--path] [--until | --for] [--uses] [--note]`, `slip list`, `slip revoke <id> | --all`, CLI only; MCP `slips`, read only (the server now lists thirteen tools). One file per slip in `slips/`, signed with a separate per-profile slip key (`keys/slips.private.jwk`); a slip that does not verify is `damaged` and never used. Uses are exclusive-create files, revoke renames the slip away first, and `clear` and the daily trim remove expired and used-up slips (never active ones, not even `--all`; `slips/` is not counted by the disk cap). `claimSlip` and `checkSlip` are ready for W-04 (forms); there is no form code yet (phase C added it). History lines gain an optional `slip` (the id) for tool `slip`.
- **Changed:** the signing key and the slip key are read by one function in `keys.ts` (same checks; the signing key's messages are unchanged). The clear report gains `slips`.
- **Round 49 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-49.md`, 1 blocking, 3 non-blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (4 mutations checked):** `slip revoke --all` and `clear` delete a use file on its own only when its slip file is confirmed gone at that moment, so a slip granted and claimed during `revoke --all` keeps its used use; `--until` with a full time checks every calendar and clock part; `--site` must be exactly an origin as written, checked before URL parsing; a bad slip key no longer blocks housekeeping (coordinator's decision): `clear` and the daily trim skip only the slip cleanup, report `slips_problem`, and trim everything else. Tests are 344.
- **Round 50 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-50.md`):** ready to merge. Banks fixed two non-blocking items: the calendar test now uses a non-leap year, and the --site error says to write an international name in its xn-- form. Accepted as outside the threat model: an id put back by hand (or a 144-bit collision) between a revoke's rename and its deletion can reset that slip's uses.
- **Tests:** 340 (14 new in `slips.test.ts`). Each was checked to fail with its fix reverted (17 mutations: the decision after the claim, the exclusive create of a use, the signature, the profile in the signed bytes, the field set, rename-first revoke, whole-segment paths, the origin match, expiry, soonest first, lowest free use, cleanup keeping active slips, the disk cap, the 200 cap, private mode for grant and for claim, and `pay`).

Phase A, built on the `v0.4-profiles` branch, not yet reviewed by Codex:

- **Added (W-18):** profiles. `default` is the store root, unchanged; other profiles live in `<root>/profiles/<name>/` (`[a-z0-9-]{1,32}`) with their own cache, history, receipts, bookmarks, handoff tickets, keys and config.json. `cooldowns.jsonl` and `safety/` stay shared at the root. `--profile <name>` on every command and `TIRESIAS_PROFILE`, fixed once per process; `tiresias mcp --profile <name>` serves one profile with no per-call parameter; replies carry top-level `profile` when it is not `default`. `tiresias profile create|list|remove` (remove needs `--yes`, and `--delete-key` for a profile with a signing key). A profile that does not exist is an error and nothing is created.
- **Changed:** every store path comes from one function (`storeBase` and `subDir` in `store.ts`); the four copies of the sub-folder helper are gone. The `default` disk cap no longer counts `profiles/` or the shared files (the cooldown log was counted before). The default Web Risk key file is used by `default` only. Web Risk verdicts in memory are kept per profile. A read on a missing profile fails with a message naming it.
- **Round 46 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-46.md`, 2 blocking, 1 non-blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix (6 mutations checked):** `profile remove` now renames the profile folder away atomically first (`profiles/.removing-<random>`) and deletes inside it, lstat-checking every folder before entering it and unlinking a link instead of following it (a folder swapped for a link mid-delete could send unlinks into another profile); leftovers are reported by `profile list` and finished by the next remove. Every folder made under a profile uses a non-recursive mkdir (`cache/` was recursive), so a profile removed between a writer's checks and its write is not recreated: the write fails. The legacy cooldown store is read only through a validated root `cache/`.
- **Round 47 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-47.md`, 1 blocking, 1 non-blocking), both fixed with tests that fail without their fix (4 mutations checked):** the signing-key decision is now made on the renamed folder, after the rename; a refused profile is renamed back, or left whole as `.removing-*` with recovery instructions when its name was taken meanwhile. The key is deleted first, and leftover cleanup never deletes a leftover that holds a key (beyond the coordinator's plan: otherwise the next removal of any profile would delete that refused key). `profile remove` finishes leftovers before anything else, even for a profile that does not exist, and says how many.
- **Tests:** 326 (20 new in `v04.test.ts`). Each new test was checked to fail with its fix reverted (13 mutations: path choice, shared entries, disk cap, key file, verdict cache, reply stamp in the engine and in MCP, the existence check, fixed selection, the link check and the key confirmation in remove).
- **Known limits:** removing a profile that a running process still serves makes that process's later writes fail (the folder is not recreated) rather than stopping the process.

### v0.3.0 (2026-10-17)

Released: needs report (W-12), diffs (W-02), bookmarks and watchlist (W-16), handoff and resume (W-06), shared cache (W-03), Safe Browsing (W-19), after Codex round 45 found nothing blocking. The entries below are the work log kept while v0.3 was built.


Built on the `v0.3-memory`, `v0.3-handoff` and `v0.3-safety` branches, all merged.

- **Added (W-12):** `tiresias needs [--since] [--page]` and the MCP tool `needs_report`. Five patterns from the history (walls by site and reason, questions that came back empty, a better door left unused, pages read most often, often-visited sites with no agent door), each a top-20 list with exact counts and a fixed-text suggestion naming the related want or need. Built when asked, never stored.
- **Changed (W-12):** history records gain optional `reason`, `matched`, `best_door` and `llms_txt`, bounded on write and read. Older records stay valid.
- **Added (W-02):** `check` returns what changed when a page changed: added and removed blocks, added ones with receipts, exact counts, paged; `read` does the same with `changes: true` (`--changes`). The list is kept with the cached page so `check --page N` can read later pages. Caps: 5,000 blocks a side, 2,000 changed blocks, 2 MiB of changed text (past them, `changes_omitted`). The diff runs in the worker under the parse budget, before the cache is replaced.
- **Changed (W-02):** `check` logs receipts when it returns added blocks, and takes `page`. Its no-store message no longer claims the page was cached.
- **Added (W-16):** bookmarks (`tiresias bookmark add|remove|watch|list`, MCP `bookmarks` and `bookmark`), one file each in `bookmarks/`, cap 1,000; and the watchlist (`tiresias watch [--page]`, MCP `watch`), which checks watched bookmarks, reports the changed ones with what changed, and names the walled, failed and waiting ones (at most 100) so a person can act on them. No scheduler: run it from cron or launchd.
- **Changed:** private mode refuses bookmark changes; `clear` and the disk cap leave bookmarks alone. The MCP server lists ten tools. Tests are 245 (31 new in `v03.test.ts`).
- **W-09:** submitted to Cloudflare as Direct on 2026-10-17, waiting for review.
- **Added (W-06):** handoff tickets. A read that stops at a wall keeps a ticket (`handoffs/<id>.json`, 7 days, `keep_handoff_days`, at most 500) and returns `handoff.ticket` with fixed text on how to resume. `tiresias resume <ticket> [--from-file <path>]` and MCP `resume` (`ticket`, optional `text`) retry the same read honestly or parse the page a person saved with no request to the site (`source: "provided_by_person"`, not cached, not a baseline). `tiresias handoffs` and MCP `handoffs` list open tickets. `clear` and the daily trim remove expired tickets; the needs report counts tickets kept, refused at the cap, and resolved by retry or by a person (not expired ones).
- **Added (W-03):** the shared cache. A cached copy the site allowed to be reused (`max-age` only, at most an hour, read with no redirect; anything unusual blocks reuse until a new 200) is served with no request to the site; replies carry `cache` (`fresh`, `revalidated`, `refetched`) and, inside `untrusted_content`, `cached_by` and `cached_at`. `fresh: true` (`--fresh`) on `read` and `check` always asks the site. The one-line repeat answer goes only to the agent that fetched the copy. A two-process test covers concurrent writes. Cross-machine sharing is not in v0.3.
- **Changed:** history lines gain optional `ticket`, `source` and `cache`; `clear` reports `handoffs`; `config.json` takes `keep_handoff_days`. The MCP server lists twelve tools. Tests were 261 after phase B (16 new in `v03b.test.ts`), 268 after round 38, 272 after round 39.
- **Round 38 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-38.md`, 7 blocking, 2 non-blocking), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix:** freshness reuse rebuilt to be conservative instead of patched (only `max-age`, capped at an hour, age from the request start, `Age` must be one plain number, no `Expires` or `Date`, sticky `reuse_blocked` that a 304 cannot clear); only a copy read with no redirect is served fresh (closes the cross-origin rules bypass); `fresh` and resume retries never reuse a probe body; every reply budget counts UTF-8 bytes (CJK tests for read, handoffs, history, receipts, log, needs and watch); `clear` counts ticket bytes in `total_bytes_freed`; the oversized MCP input test uses a live ticket. Tests are 268.
- **Round 39 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-39.md`, 4 blocking; the three round 38 deviations judged acceptable), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix:** probe-body reuse removed entirely (closes the redirect-target cooldown bypass and the roll-back after `fresh`); cached pages record `redirected` from the fetch itself and only `redirected: false` is reused fresh (an A to B to A chain no longer qualifies); a 304 never keeps an old deadline, only its own `max-age` and `Age` count; the oversized-reply fallback omits a caller URL over 2,048 bytes (`url_omitted`), and a bad `since` is echoed at most 40 characters. Tests are 272.
- **Added (W-19, on `v0.3-safety`, not yet reviewed by Codex):** the Safe Browsing check. Owner decision (Travis, 2026-10-17): Phishing.Database by default, Google Web Risk with the owner's own key optional. Every URL is checked before anything is sent to it (the asked URL first, even before a fresh cached copy is served; then each new host a call reaches, redirects, robots.txt and probes included). A list match is `status: "flagged"` (nothing fetched; `allow_flagged: true` / `--allow-flagged` reads it and keeps the flag); a Web Risk hit is `refused` and cannot be overridden. Replies carry `safety` and, for a hit, `flag` codes. The list is downloaded at most daily (conditional, signed, 32 MB cap) into `safety/`, normalized in the worker, and searched as sorted bytes (about 13 MB, a 74 ms load once per process); a copy up to 7 days old is used when the list host is down (`list_stale`), and browsing never stops for it (`unchecked`). Web Risk verdicts are cached in memory only; the key never appears in any output. New: `tiresias safety status`, `tiresias safety update`, config `safe_browsing` and `web_risk_key_file`, `TIRESIAS_SAFE_BROWSING=0`, history `flag`, needs report `counts.flagged`. Live check (2026-10-17): Google's test page `http://testsafebrowsing.appspot.com/s/malware.html` was refused by Web Risk (`web_risk:MALWARE`), `https://example.com` read `ok` with `safety: "checked"`. Then, at the coordinator's request before Codex review: Web Risk is asked about every distinct URL requested (each hop, full URL) instead of once per host, a name Tiresias would refuse (internal DNS answers, IP literals, single labels, `.local`, `.internal`, `.lan`, `.home.arpa`, `.localhost`) is never sent to Google, and a Web Risk failure is not retried within the same call. Tests are 297 (25 new in `safebrowsing.test.ts`, each checked against a mutation of the code it covers).
- **Round 44 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-44.md`, 1 blocking, 1 non-blocking, both from round 43's stop-the-download-when-nobody-waits mechanism):** a call joining a download that had just been stopped got no list (`unchecked`), and parsing ignored the stop. Coordinator's decision: remove the mechanism instead of patching it. The shared download always runs to completion and is never aborted; callers still stop at their own cancellation or deadline (round 42). Round 43's exit-time item is accepted as a documented cost: a CLI process whose call gave up during a stalled download may take up to 30 seconds to exit. Round 43's exit-time test is replaced by one where a call joins right after the starting call gave up and gets the refreshed list (flagged); it fails on the round 43 code. Tests are 306.
- **Round 43 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-43.md`, 1 blocking, 1 non-blocking, on the round 42 fixes), both fixed with tests that fail without their fix:** the generic suffix rule now needs a two-letter country code as the second label (`gov.com` is an ordinary domain and is flagged when listed), and a suffix entry is kept and flags its own exact host while never flagging names under it (`com.es` flags `com.es`, not `shop.com.es`; `ignored_suffixes` became `suffix_entries`); the shared list download is stopped when its last waiting call leaves, so the process exits at once (tested by natural exit time in a child process). Tests are 306.
- **Round 42 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-42.md`, 1 blocking, 1 non-blocking, on the round 41 fixes), both fixed with tests that fail without their fix:** a reader waiting on another call's shared list refresh now stops at its own cancellation or deadline, and the refresh runs outside every call so it goes on for the others (tested through `engine.read` with a slow list host and concurrent readers); two-label names with a generic first label (`com.es`, `gob.mx`) count as public suffixes. Tests are 304.
- **Round 41 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-41.md`, 3 blocking, 2 non-blocking, on W-19), all fixed with tests that fail without their fix:** the test-only list and Web Risk overrides accept only this machine (an https override elsewhere could have received the key); the list refresh leaves no unhandled rejection (a discarded `.finally()` chain ended the process; tested in a child process); the Web Risk step's DNS check races the call's cancellation and deadline and shares the 5 second bound with the request; list entries that are common public suffixes (`co.uk`, `github.io`, about 90 built in) are ignored and counted, never matched; README now says exactly what leaves the machine. Tests are 302.
- **Round 36 (`reviews/2026-10-17-codex-round-36.md`, 3 blocking, 3 non-blocking), all fixed with tests:** watch now shows the start of `check`'s page 1 and uses `check`'s page numbers; the watch reply always fits 60 KB (attention URLs bounded by size, omissions counted); omitted diffs carry no totals (absent, not 0); no-store answers never serve a kept list; `watch --text` names changed and attention bookmarks; MCP `history` records moved inside `untrusted_content` (and `receipts` page numbers to the top). Tests are 245.

### v0.2.0 (2026-10-17)

Released: signing (N-07), the daily log (W-13), private mode (W-14), storage limits (W-15) and PDFs (W-17), merged to main after Codex round 35 found nothing. **W-09:** submitted to Cloudflare's Bots and Agents Directory (BotBase) on 2026-10-17 as Tiresias, operator Raibit, Direct, category Agent, content use immediate, Web Bot Auth with the raibit.ai key directory. Status: waiting for review. The entries below are the work log kept while v0.2 was built.

- **Live (2026-10-16):** Raibit's key directory is served at `https://raibit.ai/.well-known/http-message-signatures-directory` (pre-signed on the Mac, keyid `4ajFcrw4Yzr_zZq5bppeehjx4dKc0gRtxP1ud08L6zA`, response signature expires 2026-11-15 15:23 UTC: re-run `keys sign-directory` and redeploy before then). The temporary page is at `https://raibit.ai/tiresias/`. Raibit's own copy has `signature_agent` set and signs every request. Cloudflare's research debug endpoint still challenges the signed request: expected until Cloudflare registration (W-09).
- **Added (W-13):** `tiresias log [--day] [--to] [--page]` and the MCP tool `log`. A daily log page generated from history and receipts on request, never stored. `--to` writes one `tiresias-log-YYYY-MM-DD.md` into an existing folder and says when it replaced an earlier export.
- **Added (W-14):** private mode. `private: true` on MCP `read`, `doors` and `check`, `--private` on the CLI, `TIRESIAS_PRIVATE=1` for a session. No history, receipts, page cache or discovery cache is read or written. Cooldowns are still honored and recorded (host and deadline only). The envelope carries `"private": true`.
- **Added (W-15):** `config.json` limits, `tiresias clear [--older-than] [--all] [--dry-run]`, a once-a-day automatic trim (checked again each local day) and a disk cap. History and receipts are one file per day and cleanup only deletes whole files.
- **Codex round 11 fixes (8 blocking, 5 non-blocking):**
  - Design change: no log is ever rewritten (findings 5, 6, 10, 11). History and receipts are one file per day; retention and the cap delete whole files, oldest first across both. Legacy v0.1 files are still read and never rewritten.
  - Design change: cooldowns are never compacted, and the pid-file mechanism (`presence.ts`, `run/`) is deleted (findings 2, 3, and the pid part of 8).
  - 1: private calls no longer read or write the shared probe-body map or the shared robots cache.
  - 4: export builds the text first, resolves the folder once, uses an exclusive temp file and refuses a symlinked final name.
  - 7: the automatic trim is keyed by store and local date, so a long-running server trims every day.
  - 8, 13: private calls, `clear --dry-run`, `log` and the history and receipts tools never create the store or change folder modes.
  - 9: config keys are checked as own properties (`constructor`, `__proto__` and similar are rejected).
  - 12: the daily log lists agents one per line (top 50) and the pager splits any over-long line.
  - `--older-than` takes whole days only.
- **Changed (round 9 item 3):** expanded JSON no longer builds the whole pretty-printed text before cutting it. `boundedPretty` (`src/reader.ts`) is a small iterative printer that gives the same bytes as `JSON.stringify(x, null, 2)` but writes at most 5 MiB (`MAX_EXPANDED`) and, for the rest, only adds up the lengths. `text_omitted` is therefore still the exact count of characters cut, now worked out by arithmetic instead of by building the string. One exception: nesting deeper than 100,000 levels (a 5 MB document can nest 2.5 million) stops the walk to bound its own stack; then `text_omitted` is a lower bound and the result also carries `text_omitted_at_least: true`. The 94,001-byte nested document from round 8 is exact and is counted in a process limited to 32 MB of heap.
- **Added (W-17):** PDFs (round 28 and 29 fixes: separate child process with an RSS watchdog that fails closed (no memory watching, no PDFs; Windows cannot read PDFs), a parent-death watchdog thread, results size-checked in the child, VmRSS in kB on Linux, parser version 7, generated page headings are never receipts, exact MIME detection, exact omitted-text counting, Node 22.13.0). A PDF (by `application/pdf`, or a `%PDF-` start) reads as one section per page, with receipts that carry the page number, the metadata title, and the usual bounds (500 pages, 5 MiB of text, both counted). pdf.js runs locked down in its own memory-watched child process; links, attachments, forms and scripts in a PDF are ignored. A damaged or password protected PDF is a clear error. Parser version 7. See the decisions in section 9 for the options and why.
- **Added (N-07):** Web Bot Auth signing. `tiresias keys init|show|directory`; `signature_agent` (and `signature_agent_style`) in config.json; with a key and a `signature_agent`, every request and redirect hop carries `Signature`, `Signature-Input` and `Signature-Agent` (the library `web-bot-auth` 0.2.0). Envelopes that sent a request carry `signed: true|false`. A damaged key or bad config fails the call; nothing is sent unsigned by accident. Tests verify real signatures with the library's `verify()`. Not built: Cloudflare registration (W-09).
- **Codex round 16 fixes (4 blocking, 2 non-blocking), plus Banks's two:**
  - The public key is derived from the private seed and the stored `x` must match (a damaged file could otherwise print the seed).
  - Only an absent key means "signing off"; every other problem fails the call. Storage checks re-run on every signed request.
  - `signature_agent` is an origin only (no path or query), in both styles.
  - `keys init` tightens an existing `keys/` folder to 0700.
  - `signed` counts only requests actually transmitted.
  - The synced-folder refusal now judges the real path against real sync roots and exact folder names, not a substring.
  - New: `tiresias keys sign-directory [--host] [--days 1..90] [--out]` makes the pre-signed directory response (body plus Content-Type, Signature, Signature-Input), prints the expiry and a renewal reminder, and never prints the private key. (Since 2026-10-27 Cloudflare needs the directory signed live, as raibit.ai now does from a Cloudflare Worker; this static copy is for hosts that cannot sign live.)
- **Codex round 12 fixes (3 blocking, 4 non-blocking):**
  - 1: `cache/`, `history/` and `receipts/` are validated (real folder, owned by us, not a link) before every read, write and deletion, and each deletion checks the file is a plain file.
  - 2: day files are named by UTC date; retention and the daily log no longer depend on the timezone (tests for a timezone change, Kiritimati, and 23 and 25 hour days).
  - 3: a private cooldown write creates only the store root and the cooldown line; it never makes `cache/` or chmods anything.
  - 4: a day folder made by another process first is accepted after a recheck; a receipts write failure never fails the browsing call.
  - 5: an append starts on a fresh line after an unterminated last line, and writes in a full-write loop.
  - 6: recorded fields are bounded (`cut: true`).
  - 7: README and SPEC no longer mention cooldown compaction, pid files or local-date file names.
- **Codex round 13 fixes (no blocking, 3 non-blocking):**
  - Listing history or receipts now checks the `history/` and `receipts/` folders (real folder, owned by us, not a link). A bad one is a clear error for `clear`, `log` and the history and receipts tools, and `clear` deletes nothing.
  - Every append starts with a newline, like cooldown appends. Readers skip blank lines, so counts and paging are unchanged.
  - The field bounds are also applied when reading, so an oversized record in a legacy `history.jsonl` or `receipts.jsonl` (or any day file) is returned cut, with `cut: true`, and stays readable over MCP.
- **Changed:** the MCP server lists six tools. Tests are 211, including 18 in `signing.test.ts` (`v02.test.ts` holds the v0.2 tests, one updated in `mcp.test.ts`, others updated to read day files).
- **Done for v0.2.0:** the signed directory is live on raibit.ai (renewed every few weeks), the Cloudflare submission is in (W-09), and the branches were re-reviewed (rounds 25, 27 and 35).
- **Known limits:** the daily log reads one day's history into memory. A local process that swaps folders you own during an export is outside the threat model. `cooldowns.jsonl` only grows.
- **Round 14 (scoped, `reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-14.md`):** ready to merge. Two slips fixed by Banks: history and receipts filters now match the stored values before the returned copy is cut, and a stray non-record line (such as `null`) is skipped instead of failing the query. Test added.

- **Round 17 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-17.md`):** all six round-16 fixes verified. Banks fixed the four new findings: Dropbox account folders such as "Dropbox (Personal)" and the folders listed in `~/.dropbox/info.json` count as sync roots; a child folder whose name starts with `..` is correctly inside its root; `sign-directory` normalizes the host the way a verifier sees it (default port 443 and leading zeros dropped); root comparisons ignore case only on macOS and Windows.
- **Round 18 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-18.md`):** the case rule was still wrong both ways (case-insensitive Linux folders, case-sensitive Mac volumes). Banks replaced name comparison with filesystem identity: a store is inside a sync root when one of its existing parent folders IS that root (same device and inode), whatever the spelling, case or links. `~/.dropbox/info.json` is read only when it is a regular file under 64 KB, and only absolute paths in it count.
- **Round 19 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-19.md`):** identity alone missed sync roots that do not exist yet. The check now refuses when EITHER the name test or the identity test says inside (see section 9). Dropbox's file is checked and read through one open file, at most 64 KB. Bind mounts of a subfolder are a documented limit.
- **Round 20 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-20.md`):** the name test now folds Unicode spellings too (composed and decomposed accents, compatibility forms, final sigma), so a missing root spelled another way is still refused. Dropbox's file is opened non-blocking, so a planted pipe cannot hang `keys init`.
- **Round 21 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-21.md`, cut off by OpenAI's content filter):** a folder name such as fullwidth "．．" folds to ".." and was read as a step up. The name test now compares folder names one by one.
- **Round 22 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-22.md`):** a configured root such as `/safe/link/../Vault` was simplified before the link was followed. Paths are now resolved one folder at a time, following each link before the next `..`, the way the system does.
- **Round 23 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-23.md`):** four more ways to combine `..`, relative paths, Windows drive forms and links. Same lesson as the cooldown lock: remove the moving part. The check now judges only plain full paths with no `..`; a store folder or sync-root setting that is relative, has no drive, or contains `..` is refused at `keys init` with a message asking for a plain path. Existing folders are resolved by the system's own `realpath`.
- **Round 24 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-24.md`):** on macOS and Linux a backslash is part of a folder name, so the check now splits paths only on that system's separators. Every remaining path resolution in the store, the daily-log export and the CLI uses the system's own `realpath` too.
- **Round 25 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-25.md`, final pass over the whole signing branch):** one blocker: a pipe planted where the key file belongs could hang the process. Every file Tiresias opens is now opened non-blocking (`NONBLOCK` in `store.ts`), then checked to be a regular file. No other blockers in the branch.
- **Round 27 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-27.md`):** the bounded JSON printer is ready to merge. Banks bumped the parser version to 5 so pages cached before it are read again.
- **Round 30 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-30.md`):** a PDF answer could be accepted while a memory check of the child was still in flight and later failed. Banks's fix: the child stays alive after answering until the parent has checked it; an answer is accepted only after the in-flight check settles, one more check of the child succeeds under the cap, and the child's own peak memory (maxRSS) is under the cap too, so a spike between checks is caught. A PDF request cancelled while it waits for a slot now leaves the queue at once.
- **Round 31 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-31.md`):** a child that died during that final check could still be approved by a reading taken before it died. The child never exits on its own any more, so any exit before the parent finishes now fails the request at once, even mid-check.
- **Round 32 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-32.md`):** the exit event can arrive after the last reading, so "any exit fails" was itself a race. Banks wrote the rule down instead (section 9): dying before the answer fails at once, dying after a complete answer does not undo it. A send that throws at once now cleans up the child, timers and listeners. The parent-death test waits for a handshake instead of fixed sleeps.
- **Round 33 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-33.md`):** no production findings; 17 real-child scenarios confirmed the acceptance rule. Two test fixes: the parent-death helper reports a failed start and the handshake is bounded (15 s), and the "dies after answering" case now really kills the child after its answer and waits for the exit event.
### Codex round 9 result (2026-10-16)

- **Verdict:** ready for a private push to main. No blocking findings. Review saved at `reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-9.md`. All six round-8 items verified fixed.
- **Fixed before the push (item 1):** a `no-store` response now removes the old cached copy at once, even if building the answer then fails. In v0.1.8 as first built, a failed search on a `no-store` page left the old copy in place.
- **Docs corrected (items 2 and 4):** the cache decision now names its one exception (a missing section still saves the page), and the 512 MB worker limit is described as an old-generation limit that Node's own flags can override.
- **Round 10 (scoped re-check, `reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-10.md`):** ready. It found one more path: a `no-store` 200 whose parsing failed still kept the old copy. The removal now happens as soon as the response arrives, before parsing or any early return. Test added.
- **Known limit (item 3), fixed in v0.2 (unreleased):** the 5 MiB cap used to be applied after the expansion, so a small nested document briefly used about 50 MB. The expansion now stops at the cap (see the v0.2 work in progress entry).

### v0.1.8 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Closes the six non-blocking items from Codex review round 8 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-8.md`: no blocking findings, ready for a private push).

- **Fixed (round 8 item 1):** worker or inline is now chosen by everything a passage search walks (`searchable_chars`: readable text plus the JSON-LD section), not just `text_chars`. A page that is all JSON-LD now searches in the worker.
- **Fixed (item 2):** a page is saved to the cache only after the answer is built. A passage search that times out no longer leaves a cached page behind while the message says "Nothing was saved".
- **Fixed (item 3):** a worker that exits without an `error` event now fails the active job at once with a worker-failure error, instead of waiting for the time budget. The next job spawns a fresh worker and works.
- **Fixed (item 4):** the parser version is now 4, so v0.1.6 and v0.1.7 caches are read again (this picks up the long-destination markdown fix).
- **Added (item 5):** expanded JSON text is capped at 5 MiB. What is cut is counted in `text_omitted` in the result, never silent.
- **Added (item 6):** the worker runs with a 512 MB `maxOldGenerationSizeMb` (`TIRESIAS_WORKER_HEAP_MB`). A worker that runs out of heap becomes `page_too_complex` and the parent keeps working.
- **Changed:** tests are now 105 (6 new in `round8.test.ts`).
- **Remaining (documented limits):** unchanged from v0.1.7 except that memory exhaustion in the worker is now contained. Robots parsing, llms discovery scanning, price parsing, cache serialization, hashing and response building still run on the main thread. Process-wide memory exhaustion is still possible, since Node cannot promise to survive it. A passage search over a 5 MiB page still runs to the time budget.

### Codex round 8 result (2026-10-16)

- **Verdict:** ready for a private push to main as a v0.1 developer preview. No blocking findings. Review saved at `reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-8.md`.
- **Docs corrected:** the worker covers the main parsing work, not all CPU work (see the D4 correction above).
- **Non-blocking, closed in v0.1.8:** (1) choose worker vs inline by all searchable content, JSON-LD included; (2) a search timeout must not leave the page cached; (3) reject promptly when a worker exits without an error; (4) bump the parser version so v0.1.6 caches rebuild; (5) bound the size of worker result messages (a 94 KB nested JSON expanded to about 48 MB of text); (6) consider Worker `resourceLimits`.

### v0.1.7 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 7 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-7.md`: three blocking, two non-blocking).

- **Fixed, blocking, and the class behind them:**
  - D4 (the class): the main CPU work on untrusted content now runs under a hard time budget in a `worker_threads` Worker (`sandbox.ts`, `work.ts`, `worker.ts`). That covers HTML, markdown, JSON and llms.txt parsing, the shield, page-marker wall scanning (pages, probes, robots.txt) and passage search. *Correction after Codex round 8:* robots parsing, llms discovery scanning, price parsing, cache serialization, hashing and response building still run on the main thread, and passage search over large JSON-LD can run inline; none reproduced as blocking. See the v0.1.8 plan. The main thread terminates the worker when the budget runs out (default 5 s, `TIRESIAS_PARSE_BUDGET_MS`) or the caller cancels. The result is `status: "error"` with the fixed text `page_too_complex`, nothing cached, no receipts. Inputs of 8 KB or less run inline, since even a quadratic parser is harmless at that size. One warm worker is kept between jobs.
  - D1: markdown heading assembly joins spans and labels once instead of copying per link (`"# " + "[a](b)" x 100,000` went from 3.5 s to well under a second).
  - D2: the shield no longer uses a regex for `!important`; it is a linear search (a 100,000 space style value took 5.3 s).
  - D3: header challenges (`cf-mitigated`) are checked on every hop's headers, including a 304, before a revalidation is accepted. A challenged 304 now returns a wall and never the cached content.
- **Fixed, non-blocking:** a challenged robots redirect honors `no-store` (call-local only), and a long markdown destination is counted in `links_omitted` and left out of the text.
- **Remaining (documented limits):**
  - N6: a Retry-After over 10 years becomes 24 hours.
  - N9: a JSON-LD record over 5 MiB is skipped (reported as `too_large`).
  - P20 and N11: if the store is reached through a symlinked alias in a writable folder, the alias's own folders are not checked.
  - The cooldown log only grows; its last 8 MB is read. Cleanup is the planned `clear` command (W-15).
  - Marker scanning looks at the first 512 KB of a page only.
  - Inputs up to 8 KB are processed inline, outside the time budget.
  - The time budget stops a slow job but not a job that exhausts memory.
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test. Link lists have no receipts. The robots budget counts steps, not milliseconds. The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, keyword-only search, no PDFs and no JavaScript are unchanged.

### v0.1.6 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 6 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-6.md`: round 5 verified fixed; four blocking items from a whole-repo pass, one small regression).

- **Fixed, blocking:**
  - C1 (regex denial of service): the CAPTCHA regex `<form[\s\S]*?captcha[\s\S]*?</form>` took 9.3 s on `"<form "` x 128,000 and could not be cancelled. All marker detection in `walls.ts` is now `indexOf` scanning over at most the first 512 KB, with fixed look-ahead limits. I audited every regex that touches web content and replaced the other risky ones: password-input detection, tag stripping for the text length, markdown comment removal, markdown link and heading parsing, and the llms.txt link scan. A test runs hostile inputs for each and checks they finish in well under a second. This also fixed a latent quadratic cost when a single paragraph was very long (whitespace collapsing).
  - C2: a 429/503 cooldown is recorded when the response headers arrive (a new `onHeaders` hook on every hop), before the body is read. A body that dies mid-way or exceeds the size cap no longer loses the Retry-After. Applies to robots.txt too.
  - C3: every hop's headers are checked before its Location is followed. A redirect carrying `cf-mitigated: challenge` stops the call as a wall, with the handoff naming that hop, for pages and for the redirect chain to robots.txt.
  - C4: a 402 on robots.txt is returned as `payment_required` with the price parsed, and nothing else is requested.
- **Fixed, small:** C5: `cooldowns.old.jsonl` from v0.1.4 is read as a legacy source (merged by latest deadline) and never written.
- **Decision confirmed (section 9):** robots.txt 401, 403 and 404 mean "no rules" (RFC 9309). Only a real challenge on robots.txt (header or page markers) is a wall. 402 is a payment demand. 429 and 5xx stop the call.
- **Remaining (documented limits):**
  - N6: a Retry-After over 10 years becomes 24 hours.
  - N9: a JSON-LD record over 5 MiB is skipped (reported as `too_large`).
  - P20 and N11: if the store is reached through a symlinked alias in a writable folder, the alias's own folders are not checked.
  - The cooldown log only grows; its last 8 MB is read. Cleanup is the planned `clear` command (W-15).
  - Marker scanning looks at the first 512 KB of a page only.
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test. Link lists have no receipts. The robots budget counts steps, not milliseconds. The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, keyword-only search, no PDFs and no JavaScript are unchanged.

### v0.1.5 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 5 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-5.md`: three blocking items in rotation, two small).

- **Fixed, blocking (rotation removed):**
  - R1 (overlapping rotations erase cooldowns), R2 (two files are not a consistent view) and R3 (the 1,000 origin carry-forward cap drops origins) are all gone with rotation itself. `cooldowns.jsonl` is one append-only file that Tiresias never renames, rewrites or truncates at runtime. `cooldowns.old.jsonl`, `MAX_LIVE` and the rotation code are deleted. See the decision in section 9.
  - Reading scans the whole file and takes the latest deadline per site, with no cap on the number of sites. A file over 8 MB is read from its last 8 MB, starting at the first full line (tested).
- **Fixed, small:**
  - R4: each cooldown record is written as `"\n" + json + "\n"`, so a damaged unterminated tail can never swallow the next record. Blank lines are skipped on read.
  - R5: the v0.1.3 JSON cooldown store is read if it exists (merged by latest deadline) and never written.
- **Removed:** the earlier residual about rotation windows no longer applies.
- **Remaining (documented limits):**
  - N6: a Retry-After over 10 years becomes 24 hours.
  - N9: a JSON-LD record over 5 MiB is skipped (reported as `too_large`).
  - P20 and N11: if the store is reached through a symlinked alias in a writable folder, the alias's own folders are not checked.
  - The cooldown log only grows (a few lines per 429/503). Beyond 8 MB, entries older than the last 8 MB are not seen. Cleanup is the planned `clear` command (W-15).
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test. Link lists have no receipts. The robots budget counts steps, not milliseconds. The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, keyword-only search, no PDFs and no JavaScript are unchanged.

### v0.1.4 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 4 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-4.md`: one blocking item, B1, plus small ones). Numbers: A = round-3 item status, B-1 and B-2 = new in round 4.

- **Fixed, blocking:**
  - B1: the cooldown lock is gone. Cooldowns are an append-only log, `cooldowns.jsonl`. Recording one appends a single short line `{origin, until}` (one atomic write on an O_APPEND fd; lines stay far under 512 bytes), and only when the deadline is later than what is already known. Reading takes the latest `until` per site from both files and ignores expired or damaged lines.
  - Rotation: past 256 KB, every still-live deadline is first copied into the current file, then it is renamed over `cooldowns.old.jsonl`. Readers always read both files, so an append that races a rotation lands in one of them. A live cooldown survives any number of rotations.
  - B-2 (a busy lock dropped cooldown persistence) is fixed by the same change: there is no busy path.
  - The in-memory cooldown in the running process stays.
- **Fixed, non-blocking:**
  - B-1: after a parser upgrade, an old cache entry keeps its source and baseline (so `check` still reads where it read from and does not say "First visit"). Only the parsed form is rebuilt; validators are not sent for that one request, and an identical body keeps its original read time.
  - B4 residual: the discovery cache is versioned (`DOORS_VERSION`), so older entries with shortened sitemap URLs are discarded.
  - P16 residual: section pages and question answers are budgeted by what is really sent, receipts (and their repeated URL) included. A 7,000 character path no longer pushes a page over the limit.
- **Remaining (documented limits):**
  - N6: a Retry-After over 10 years is replaced by 24 hours, not honored.
  - N9: a JSON-LD record over 5 MiB is skipped (reported as `too_large`) with no continuation.
  - P20 and N11: ancestors are checked along the resolved path. If the store is reached through a symlinked alias in a writable folder, the alias's own folders are not checked.
  - A cooldown append made on a file descriptor opened before a second rotation, and completed after that second rotation's scan, can be lost. The window is a single write.
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test. Link lists have no receipts. The robots budget counts steps, not milliseconds. The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, keyword-only search, no PDFs and no JavaScript are unchanged.

### v0.1.3 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 3 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-3.md`: no High; B1 to B5 new, plus residual partials).

- **Fixed, new:**
  - B1: the cooldown lock records its owner (pid, random token, creation time). A lock is broken only if it is older than 5 seconds AND its pid is dead, or older than a hard 60 seconds. Waiting is never evidence of death: a contender that waits 10 seconds gives up with a busy error, and the cooldown is still honored in memory by this process. Stale locks are moved aside with an atomic rename. A lock is released only if the token is still ours.
  - B2: the throttle checks cancellation before queueing and again before it sets the clock. The cancel listener is attached before any abort path. A cancelled entry leaves the queue at once and never delays the next request.
  - B3: an overlong heading is shown cut at 300 characters in the outline, and its full text becomes the section's first block, so it stays readable, searchable and quotable.
  - B4: URLs are never shortened. Feeds, sitemaps, service-description URLs and page links over 2,048 characters, or past a 4,000 character list budget, are left out and counted in `available_doors.omitted` and `links_omitted`.
  - B5: robots cache selection checks expiry first and deletes expired global entries.
- **Fixed, residual:**
  - P14: every section page carries the heading receipt.
  - P16 and N10: service-description and every door URL field are bounded as in B4, and a final whole-reply guard drops optional parts (`available_doors`, `links`, `structured`, `flag_details`) and lists them in `dropped_for_size`.
  - P23: x402 is validated per version. v1 needs scheme, network, `maxAmountRequired`, resource, description, mimeType, payTo, maxTimeoutSeconds and asset. v2 needs a top-level `resource.url` and, per option, scheme, network, `amount`, asset, payTo and maxTimeoutSeconds. The other version's amount field makes an option invalid. An invalid body never masks a valid header.
  - N6: nothing is shortened. A Retry-After over 10 years (seconds or date) is treated as invalid and replaced by a fixed 24 hour cooldown.
  - N9: cache entries carry a parser version and older entries are read again. Records over the 5 MiB allowance stay skipped, and `structured.skipped` now says `{ invalid, too_large }`.
  - P20 and N11: every call walks the real path of the store up to `$HOME` (not including it) or the root. An ancestor not owned by this user or root, or writable by group or others without the sticky bit, is refused.
  - P5: unchanged, a documented decision (section 9).
- **Remaining:**
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test.
  - Link lists have no receipts.
  - Lock recovery moves an abandoned lock aside by rename; if a live process replaces a lock between our read and our rename, we put it back with a hard link, which narrows but cannot fully remove that race on every filesystem.
  - The ancestor check stops at `$HOME` and trusts it.
  - The robots budget counts steps, not milliseconds.
  - The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, keyword-only search, no PDFs and no JavaScript are unchanged.

### v0.1.2 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Fixes Codex review round 2 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-2.md`: 13 fixed, 13 partial, 13 new). Numbers below: P = round-1 finding that was partial, N = new finding in round 2.

- **Fixed, new High:**
  - N1: robots matching never uses a truncated path. The full normalized path is matched; a path over 8,192 characters, or rules that cost more than the step budget, are refused (fail closed).
  - N2: `handoff.open_this` moved to `untrusted_content.handoff.open_this`. The top level keeps only the fixed `reason` code and `explanation`.
  - N3: the cooldown is rechecked right before each queued send, and queued requests for a site are cancelled the moment its cooldown starts.
- **Fixed, partial items:**
  - P3: one step budget (3,000,000) covers all rule matching for a decision.
  - P4: native error text moved into `untrusted_content.detail`; top-level messages are fixed text.
  - P5: decision recorded (see section 9). A main-page 403 is tested to still return `wall`.
  - P7: closed by N3 to N6 and N8.
  - P11: closed by N7 and N8.
  - P13: the robots wall names the page that actually walled, even after a redirect.
  - P14: every outline heading and preview, every passage heading and the title have receipts.
  - P16: every returned value is bounded (titles, headings, link text, sitemaps, feeds, question echo), so no single item can break a page budget.
  - P19: a 304 with `no-store` removes the cached copy. A `no-store` robots.txt is kept for one call only.
  - P20: closed by N11 and N12.
  - P23: x402 v1/v2 options need a known version, scheme, network, asset, payee and an integer amount string (`amount` or `maxAmountRequired`).
  - P26: closed by N13.
- **Fixed, new Medium and Low:**
  - N4: robots redirects check the cooldown on every hop, and a 429/503 is blamed on the site that sent it.
  - N5: the cooldown file is updated under a lock file with a stale timeout, merging by the longest wait.
  - N6: the full Retry-After is kept (only an absurd 10 year value is cut), and a shorter wait never replaces a longer one.
  - N7: cancellation is never read as "no door"; the call checks it before reusing a cached body and before saving anything.
  - N8: policy and queue work runs under the call's own deadline and cancellation; sleeps are abortable and cancelled jobs leave the queue.
  - N9: JSON-LD up to the fetch cap is kept and paged; invalid or oversized records are counted in `structured.skipped`.
  - N10: see P16.
  - N11: a symlinked store folder is rejected before any change, the folder is re-validated by device and inode, and a world-writable parent without the sticky bit is refused.
  - N12: rejected log files close their descriptor.
  - N13: an oversized MCP reply becomes a complete error envelope with `isError: true`.
- **Changed:** handoff shape (above), MCP replies are compact JSON, user agent is `Tiresias/0.1.2`.
- **Remaining:**
  - The HTTPS path has no local automated test.
  - Link lists have no receipts.
  - The robots step budget counts steps, not milliseconds, so wall time varies by machine (it measured well under a second in tests).
  - The store's parent check refuses only a world-writable folder without the sticky bit. It does not audit every ancestor's owner.
  - The receipts log keeps quotes from `no-store` pages.
  - Shield, passage search, PDF and JavaScript limits are unchanged from v0.1.1.

### v0.1.1 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Spec stays at 0.5 apart from section 6.1. Fixes Codex review round 1 (`reviews/2026-10-16-codex-round-1.md`, 26 findings, verdict "not ready").

- **Fixed, High:** #1 DNS rebinding (the connection now resolves through one validating lookup and uses that answer; Node `http`/`https` with a custom `lookup`, hostname kept for Host and TLS). #2 IPv6 is default-deny outside global unicast, with special-use blocks removed (site-local, local-use NAT64, Teredo, documentation, 6to4 and NAT64 checked as their IPv4). #3 robots matching uses a non-backtracking matcher with size limits (too large a robots.txt fails closed). #4 every web-derived value moved inside `untrusted_content`, receipts included. #5 a wall or 402 found by any probe stops discovery and cancels the other probes; `doors` checks the page for walls. #6 a challenged robots.txt is a wall. #7 Retry-After is enforced as a per-site cooldown stored on disk, checked before every request including probes and robots refreshes; seconds and HTTP-date both parsed. #8 robots paths and rules are normalized per RFC 9309.
- **Fixed, Medium:** #9 CR, LF and CRLF; Sitemap lines no longer split User-agent runs. #10 `cf-mitigated` is honored for any status and content type. #11 one deadline covers DNS, policy waits, redirects and body; MCP cancellation is passed through. #12 validators are sent only to the URL they came from, and a 304 from any other URL is rejected. #13 the cache stores requested, source and final URLs; receipts cite the page actually read; handoff is the exact wall URL. #14 quotes are exact substrings, markers kept separate, every returned block has a receipt. #15 truncated probe bodies are never reused as full pages. #16 result budgets and `page`, streamed log reads. #17 iterative walkers with a 200,000 element budget. #18 titles, headings and link text go through the shield. #19 `Cache-Control: no-store` is honored for page and discovery caches. #20 store is 0700/0600, owner-checked, symlinks refused, random exclusive temp files.
- **Fixed, Low and extra:** #21 JSON-LD is a numbered, searchable section. #22 JSON is kept in full and paged. #23 x402 candidates are validated, so a malformed one no longer hides a valid header. #24 size settings are validated. #25 charset comes from BOM, header or HTML meta. #26 `isError` is set on execution errors.
- **Changed:** envelope shape (see 6.1). `retry_after_seconds` and `handoff.reason` codes are new. `receipts` and `history` queries return pages. User agent is now `Tiresias/0.1.1`.
- **Known limits that remain:**
  - The HTTPS path (TLS, SNI) uses the same pinned lookup but has no local automated test, since the test server is plain HTTP.
  - A plain 401/403 on a discovery probe path (for example `/.well-known/mcp.json`) is treated as "no such door", not a wall. The page request that follows decides. Challenges, CAPTCHAs and 402 on a probe do stop everything.
  - The shield sees inline styles and attributes only. Stylesheet or script-hidden text is not detected.
  - The receipts log keeps quotes even from `no-store` pages. `no-store` governs the response caches only.
  - Link lists have no receipts, and the markdown reader treats `[text](url)` link text as the extracted text.
  - Passage search is keyword overlap. PDFs are unsupported. No JavaScript rendering. MCP, API and feed doors are reported, not used.
  - Text sizes are counted in characters, not bytes, for page budgets.

### v0.1.0 (2026-10-16)

Software release. Spec stays at 0.5.

- **Added:** the engine (safety, rules, door finder, fetcher and cache, wall detector, reader, shield, receipts, history), the MCP server (`read`, `doors`, `check`, `receipts`, `history`) and the command line (`read`, `doors`, `check`, `receipts`, `history`, `mcp`). Local store at `~/.tiresias/` (or `TIRESIAS_HOME`) holding `cache/`, `receipts.jsonl` and `history.jsonl`. 24 tests.
- **Closed:** N-02, N-03, N-04, N-05, N-06, N-08, N-09, N-10, W-01, W-11.
- **Partly closed:** N-01. All rungs are detected and reported in `available_doors`; only llms.txt/markdown, structured data and page text are consumed.
- **Known limits:**
  - MCP, API and feed doors are detected but not used to answer a read.
  - The shield sees inline styles and attributes only. Text hidden by a stylesheet or by JavaScript is not detected.
  - (Fixed in v0.1.1) Safe fetching checked the DNS answer, then `fetch` resolved again.
  - Reader handles HTML, markdown, plain text and JSON. PDFs and other binaries return an error (W-17).
  - Passage search is keyword overlap, not meaning.
  - A page with a CAPTCHA widget or a password field is called a wall only when it has little other text, to avoid false alarms on real articles.
  - `Crawl-delay` is honored up to 30 seconds; a longer delay makes the call refuse.
  - Robots rules match raw paths; percent-encoding differences are not normalized.
  - No JavaScript rendering, no request signing (N-07), no private mode (W-14).

## Credits and prior art

Studied for ideas, never copied:

- **Lynx** (1992): proof that a browser without eyes works; pages as plain text and numbered links.
- **Lightpanda**: a headless browser built from scratch for automation (AGPL-3.0; studied, not used).
- **Stealth agent browsers**: studied as the model of what not to do.
- Standards: [Web Bot Auth drafts (IETF)](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-webbotauth-httpsig-protocol), [llms.txt](https://llmstxt.org/), [Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/), HTTP 402 and x402.
